\mathscr{K}_{1}^{1/2}\mathscr{V}_{12\mid 3}\mathscr{K}_{2}^{1/2}=0
. Therefore, from the result of Lemma
A.4
, we could establish the following equivalence: for all
f,g\in\mathbb{H}
,

\mathscr{V}_{12\mid 3}(f,g)=0,\;\mathscr{V}_{21\mid 3}(f,g)=0\Longleftrightarrow\mathscr{K}_{12\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\bullet$}}}}3}(f,g)=0,\;\mathscr{K}_{21\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\bullet$}}}}3}(f,g)=0,

which proves the first statement.
It remains to show that, under the Gaussian setting, the following equivalence holds

\mathcal{Z}_{1}\perp\!\!\!\perp\mathcal{Z}_{2}\mid\mathcal{Z}_{3}\Longleftrightarrow\mathscr{K}_{12\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\bullet$}}}}3},\mathscr{K}_{21\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\bullet$}}}}3}\text{ are in the equivalent class of the zero operator}.

Note that under the Gaussian assumption, by Lemma
A.6
, the conditional estimator is the linear estimator. Then,
one could look at residual element
\mathcal{Z}_{i\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\bullet$}}}}3}
as the residual as
\mathcal{Z}_{i\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\bullet$}}}}3}=\mathcal{Z}_{i}-\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{Z}_{i}\mid\mathcal{Z}_{3}]
for
i=1,2
. In addition, since
\mathcal{Z}_{i}
for
i=1,2,3
are zero mean, the mean elements
\mathfrak{m}_{i\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\bullet$}}}}3}
for
i=1,2
are zero elements. Consequently, for any
f,g\in\mathbb{H}
, we could write

\displaystyle\langle{f},{\mathscr{K}_{12\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\bullet$}}}}3}g}\rangle_{\mathbb{H}}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\langle{\mathcal{Z}_{1}-\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{Z}_{1}\mid\mathcal{Z}_{3}]},{f}\rangle_{\mathbb{H}}\langle{\mathcal{Z}_{2}-\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{Z}_{2}\mid\mathcal{Z}_{3}]},{g}\rangle_{\mathbb{H}}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{Z}_{3}}\big[\underbrace{\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{Z}_{1},\mathcal{Z}_{2}\mid\mathcal{Z}_{3}}\left[\langle{\mathcal{Z}_{1}-\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{Z}_{1}\mid\mathcal{Z}_{3}]},{f}\rangle_{\mathbb{H}}\langle{\mathcal{Z}_{2}-\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{Z}_{2}\mid\mathcal{Z}_{3}]},{g}\rangle_{\mathbb{H}}\mid\mathcal{Z}_{3}\right]}_{=:\mathscr{T}(f,g)}\big].

(A.4)

By definition, if
\mathcal{Z}_{1}\perp\!\!\!\perp\mathcal{Z}_{2}\mid\mathcal{Z}_{3}
, for any
f,g\in\mathbb{H}
, we have
\mathscr{T}(f,g)=0
, which implies that
\mathscr{K}_{12\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\bullet$}}}}3}=0
. Conversely, under the Gaussian setting, the conditional covariance does not depend on the value of
\mathcal{Z}_{3}
. Therefore, we can ignore the outer expectation of (
A.4
) and see that
\mathscr{K}_{12\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\bullet$}}}}3}=0
implies
\mathcal{Z}_{1}\perp\!\!\!\perp\mathcal{Z}_{2}\mid\mathcal{Z}_{3}
.

A.4
Proof of Theorem
2.2

The proof step follows similarly to the proof step of the second statement of Theorem
A.3
. Noting that, we could generalize the argument of Lemma
A.5
to a set of random element
\{\mathcal{Z}_{1},\ldots,\mathcal{Z}_{d}\}
and show that the space

\left\{\sum_{j^{\prime}\in\{1,\ldots,p\}\backslash\{i,j\}}\beta_{j^{\prime}}\mathcal{Z}_{j^{\prime}}:\beta_{j^{\prime}}\in\mathfrak{B}(\mathbb{H},\mathbb{H}),j^{\prime}\in\{1,\ldots,p\}\backslash\{i,j\}\right\}\\
\subseteq L^{2}(\Omega,\sigma(\mathcal{Z}_{j^{\prime}};j^{\prime}\in\{1,\ldots,p\}\backslash\{i,j\}),\mu),

is linear and closed, following a similar proof step of Lemma
A.5
. By the orthogonality principle of Hilbert space (see
Conway 2007
for an exposition), we have

{
Z
_i-∑_j’∈{1,…,p}\{i,j}
~

β

_ij’
Z
_j’}
⟂
Z
_j’, j∈{1,…,p}\{i,j}. Then, under the jointly Gaussian assumption, we could write

\displaystyle\mathcal{Z}_{i}-\sum_{j^{\prime}\in\{1,\ldots,p\}\backslash\{i,j\}}\widetilde{\beta}_{ij^{\prime}}\mathcal{Z}_{j^{\prime}}=\mathcal{Z}_{i}-\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{Z}_{i}\mid\mathcal{Z}_{-(i,j)}];

\displaystyle\mathcal{Z}_{j}-\sum_{j^{\prime}\in\{1,\ldots,p\}\backslash\{i,j\}}\widetilde{\beta}_{jj^{\prime}}\mathcal{Z}_{j^{\prime}}=\mathcal{Z}_{i}-\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{Z}_{j}\mid\mathcal{Z}_{-(i,j)}],

following similar argument of Lemma
A.6
. Then, the remaining step is the same as the second part of the proof of Theorem
A.3
, where it is left to verify the condition: for any
f,g\in\mathbb{H}

E[

⟨
Z
_i-
E
[
Z
_i∣
Z
_-(i,j)],f⟩_
H

⟨
Z
_i-
E
[
Z
_j∣
Z
_-(i,j)],g⟩_
H
∣Z_j’,j’∈{1,…,p}\{i,j}]=0,

similar as (
A.4
). Therefore, following Definition
2.1
, we can show that
\mathscr{K}_{ij{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\bullet$}}}}}}
is in the equivalence class of a zero operator if and only if
\mathcal{Z}_{i}\perp\!\!\!\perp\mathcal{Z}_{j}\mid\mathcal{Z}_{-(i,j)}
for
(i,j)\not\in E
.

A.5
Proof of Corollary
2.3

The first statement is easy to verify as we can apply the orthogonality principle of the Hilbert space and obtain

\mathcal{Z}_{i}=\sum_{j^{\prime}\in{V\backslash\{i\}}}\beta_{ij^{\prime}}^{\star}\mathcal{Z}_{j^{\prime}}+\mathscr{W}_{i},\quad\mathcal{Z}_{j}=\sum_{j^{\prime}\in\{1,\ldots,p\}\backslash\{j\}}\beta_{jj^{\prime}}^{\star}\mathcal{Z}_{j^{\prime}}+\mathscr{W}_{j},

where
\mathscr{W}_{i}
is independent of
\mathcal{Z}_{j^{\prime}}
for
j^{\prime}\in{V\backslash\{i\}}
and
\mathscr{W}_{j}
is independent of
\mathcal{Z}_{j^{\prime}}
for
j^{\prime}\in\{1,\ldots,p\}\backslash\{j\}
. Furthermore,
\mathscr{W}_{i}
and
\mathscr{W}_{j}
are centered and independent of each other.

We then prove the second statement. Here we prove an equivalent statement:
\beta_{ij}^{\star}
and
\beta_{ji}^{\star}
are in the equivalent class of zero operator if and only if
\mathcal{Z}_{i}\perp\!\!\!\perp\mathcal{Z}_{j}\mid\mathcal{Z}_{-(i,j)}
.
We first show the forward direction: if
\beta_{ij}^{\star}
and
\beta_{ji}^{\star}
are in the equivalent class of zero operator, then

\mathcal{Z}_{i}\perp\!\!\!\perp\mathcal{Z}_{j}\mid\mathcal{Z}_{-(i,j)}
.
By Theorem
2.2
, we only need to show that
\mathscr{K}_{ij\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\bullet$}}}}}
is in the equivalence class of zero operator.
For any
f,g\in\mathbb{H}
, we can write

\displaystyle\langle{\mathscr{K}_{ij{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\bullet$}}}}}}f},{g}\rangle_{\mathbb{H}}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\langle{\beta_{ij}^{\star}\mathcal{Z}_{j}+\mathscr{W}_{i}},{f}\rangle_{\mathbb{H}}\langle{\beta_{ji}^{\star}\mathcal{Z}_{i}+\mathscr{W}_{j}},{g}\rangle_{\mathbb{H}}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\langle{\mathscr{W}_{i}},{f}\rangle_{\mathbb{H}}\langle{\mathscr{W}_{j}},{g}\rangle_{\mathbb{H}}\right]=0.

To show the other direction, it suffices to show that
\mathscr{K}_{ij\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\bullet$}}}}}
is in the equivalence class of a zero operator implies that
\beta_{ij}^{\star}
in the equivalence class of a zero operator, which is equivalent to show that ⟨
E
[
Z
_i∣
Z
_-(i,j)],f⟩_
H
=⟨
E
[
Z
_i∣
Z
_j∈V\{i}],f⟩_
H
.

First, we denote the residuals of
\mathcal{Z}_{i}
and
\mathcal{Z}_{j}
on
\mathcal{Z}_{j^{\prime}}
for
j^{\prime}\in\{1,\ldots,p\}\backslash\{i,j\}
as
r_{i}
and
r_{j}
, respectively. Then for any
f\in\mathbb{H}
, we have

\displaystyle\langle{\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{Z}_{i}\mid\mathcal{Z}_{j\in{V\backslash\{i\}}}]},{f}\rangle_{\mathbb{H}}

\displaystyle=\langle{\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{Z}_{i}\mid\mathbb{E}[Z_{j}\mid\mathcal{Z}_{-(i,j)}]+r_{j},\mathcal{Z}_{-(i,j)}]},{f}\rangle_{\mathbb{H}}

\displaystyle=\langle{\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{Z}_{i}\mid r_{j},\mathcal{Z}_{-(i,j)}]},{f}\rangle_{\mathbb{H}}

\displaystyle=\langle{\mathbb{E}[\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{Z}_{i}\mid\mathcal{Z}_{-(i,j)}]\mid r_{j},\mathcal{Z}_{-(i,j)}]},{f}\rangle_{\mathbb{H}}+\langle{\mathbb{E}[r_{i}\mid r_{j},\mathcal{Z}_{-(i,j)}]},{f}\rangle_{\mathbb{H}}.

Apply the fact that
\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{Z}_{i}\mid\mathcal{Z}_{-(i,j)}]
is independent of
r_{j}
and
r_{i}
is independent of
\mathcal{Z}_{-(i,j)}
, the above display is equivalent as

\displaystyle=\langle{\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{Z}_{i}\mid\mathcal{Z}_{-(i,j)}]},{f}\rangle_{\mathbb{H}}+\langle{\mathbb{E}[r_{i}\mid r_{j}]},{f}\rangle_{\mathbb{H}}.

Since
\mathscr{K}_{ij\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\bullet$}}}}}
is in the equivalence class of zero operator, we know that the residual
r_{i}
and
r_{j}
are independent. Therefore, the above display is equivalent as

\displaystyle=\langle{\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{Z}_{i}\mid\mathcal{Z}_{-(i,j)}]},{f}\rangle_{\mathbb{H}}+\langle{\mathbb{E}[r_{i}]},{f}\rangle_{\mathbb{H}}

\displaystyle=\langle{\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{Z}_{i}\mid\mathcal{Z}_{-(i,j)}]},{f}\rangle_{\mathbb{H}}.

Hence, we complete the proof.

A.6
Auxiliary Lemmas

Lemma A.4
.

Under the conditions of Theorem
A.3
, for any
f,g\in\mathbb{H}
, the properties hold

\langle{\mathscr{K}_{12\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\bullet$}}}}3}g},{f}\rangle_{\mathbb{H}}=\langle{\mathscr{K}_{1}^{1/2}\mathscr{V}_{12\mid 3}\mathscr{K}_{2}^{1/2}g},{f}\rangle_{\mathbb{H}},\quad\langle{\mathscr{K}_{21\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\bullet$}}}}3}f},{g}\rangle_{\mathbb{H}}=\langle{\mathscr{K}_{2}^{1/2}\mathscr{V}_{21\mid 3}\mathscr{K}_{1}^{1/2}f},{g}\rangle_{\mathbb{H}}.

Proof of Lemma
A.4
.

Recall the definition of the residual element
\mathcal{Z}_{i\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\bullet$}}}}3}
in (
A.2
) and based on Lemma
A.8
, we could write
\mathcal{Z}_{i\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\bullet$}}}}3}
as
\mathcal{Z}_{i}-\beta_{i}\mathcal{Z}_{i}
for some
\beta_{i}
that satisfies
\mathscr{K}_{i3}-\beta_{i}\mathscr{K}_{3}=0
for
i=1,2
. Since
\mathcal{Z}_{i}
for
i=1,2,3
are zero-mean, it follows that the residual mean elements
\mathfrak{m}_{i\cdot 3}
for
i=1,2
are zero elements. Then, for any
f,g\in\mathbb{H}
, write

\displaystyle\langle{f},{\mathscr{K}_{12,\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\bullet$}}}}3}g}\rangle_{\mathbb{H}}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}[\langle{\mathcal{Z}_{1\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\bullet$}}}}3}},{f}\rangle_{\mathbb{H}}\langle{\mathcal{Z}_{2\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\bullet$}}}}3}},{g}\rangle_{\mathbb{H}}]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}[\langle{\mathcal{Z}_{1}-\beta_{1}\mathcal{Z}_{3}},{f}\rangle_{\mathbb{H}}\langle{\mathcal{Z}_{2}-\beta_{2}\mathcal{Z}_{3}},{g}\rangle_{\mathbb{H}}]

\displaystyle=\langle{f},{(\mathscr{K}_{12}+\beta_{1}\mathscr{K}_{3}\beta_{2}^{*}-\beta_{1}\mathscr{K}_{32}-\mathscr{K}_{13}\beta_{2}^{*})g}\rangle_{\mathbb{H}},

where
\beta_{2}^{*}
is the adjoint operator of
\beta_{2}
. Apply the fact that
\beta_{1}\mathscr{K}_{3}=\mathscr{K}_{13}
. The above display is equivalent as

\displaystyle=\langle{f},{(\mathscr{K}_{12}-\beta_{1}\mathscr{K}_{32})g}\rangle_{\mathbb{H}}.

Next, write
\mathscr{K}_{1}^{1/2}\mathscr{V}_{12\mid 3}\mathscr{K}_{2}^{1/2}=\mathscr{K}_{12}-\mathscr{K}_{1}^{1/2}\mathscr{V}_{13}\mathscr{V}_{32}\mathscr{K}_{2}^{1/2}
. Then, for any
f,g\in\mathbb{H}
, we have

\displaystyle\langle{f},{\mathscr{K}_{1}^{1/2}\mathscr{V}_{12\mid 3}\mathscr{K}_{2}^{1/2}g}\rangle_{\mathbb{H}}=\langle{f},{\mathscr{K}_{12}g}\rangle_{\mathbb{H}}-\langle{f},{\mathscr{K}_{1}^{1/2}\mathscr{V}_{13}\mathscr{V}_{32}\mathscr{K}_{2}^{1/2}g}\rangle_{\mathbb{H}}.

Therefore, to show the first statement, it suffices to show that
\langle{f},{\mathscr{K}_{1}^{1/2}\mathscr{V}_{13}\mathscr{V}_{32}\mathscr{K}_{2}^{1/2}g}\rangle_{\mathbb{H}}=\langle{f},{\beta_{1}\mathscr{K}_{32}g}\rangle_{\mathbb{H}}
for any
f,g\in\mathbb{H}
.

Recall that
\Image(\mathscr{V}_{32})\subset\overline{\Image(\mathscr{K}_{3})}=\overline{\Image(\mathscr{K}_{3}^{1/2})}
and define the projection operator to

\overline{\Image(\mathscr{K}_{3}^{1/2})}
as
\mathscr{P}_{3}=\mathscr{K}_{3}^{1/2}(\mathscr{K}_{3}^{1/2})^{\dagger}
. Then, we have
\mathscr{P}_{3}\mathscr{V}_{32}=\mathscr{V}_{32}
. Therefore, we can write

\displaystyle\langle{f},{\mathscr{K}_{1}^{1/2}\mathscr{V}_{13}\mathscr{P}_{3}\mathscr{V}_{32}\mathscr{K}_{2}^{1/2}g}\rangle_{\mathbb{H}}

\displaystyle=\langle{f},{\mathscr{K}_{1}^{1/2}\mathscr{V}_{13}\mathscr{K}_{3}^{1/2}(\mathscr{K}_{3}^{1/2})^{\dagger}\mathscr{V}_{32}\mathscr{K}_{2}^{1/2}g}\rangle_{\mathbb{H}}

\displaystyle=\langle{f},{\mathscr{K}_{13}(\mathscr{K}_{3}^{1/2})^{\dagger}\mathscr{V}_{32}\mathscr{K}_{2}^{1/2}g}\rangle_{\mathbb{H}}.

Applying the fact that
\mathscr{K}_{13}=\beta_{1}\mathscr{K}_{3}
, the above display yields

\displaystyle=\langle{f},{\beta_{1}\mathscr{K}_{3}^{1/2}\mathscr{K}_{3}^{1/2}(\mathscr{K}_{3}^{1/2})^{\dagger}\mathscr{V}_{32}\mathscr{K}_{2}^{1/2}g}\rangle_{\mathbb{H}}

\displaystyle=\langle{f},{\beta_{1}\mathscr{K}_{3}^{1/2}\mathscr{P}_{3}\mathscr{V}_{32}\mathscr{K}_{2}^{1/2}g}\rangle_{\mathbb{H}}

\displaystyle=\langle{f},{\beta_{1}\mathscr{K}_{3}^{1/2}\mathscr{V}_{32}\mathscr{K}_{2}^{1/2}g}\rangle_{\mathbb{H}}

\displaystyle=\langle{f},{\beta_{1}\mathscr{K}_{32}g}\rangle_{\mathbb{H}}.

Hence, we complete the proof.
∎

Lemma A.5
.

Let
\mathcal{Z}\in L^{2}(\Omega,\mathcal{F},\mu)
be a centered random element taking values in the measurable Hilbert space
(\mathbb{H},\mathscr{B}(\mathbb{H}))
, where
\mathbb{H}
is a separable Hilbert space. Define the space

V=\{\beta\mathcal{Z}:\beta\in\mathfrak{B}(\mathbb{H},\mathbb{H})\},

where
\mathfrak{B}(\mathbb{H},\mathbb{H})
is the space of all linear bounded operator mapping to its own Hilbert space
\mathbb{H}
.
Then,
V
is a closed linear subspace of
L^{2}(\Omega,\sigma(\mathcal{Z}),\mu)
.

Proof of Lemma
A.5
.

First, we verify that
V\subseteq L^{2}(\Omega,\sigma(\mathcal{Z}),\mu)
.
Since
\beta
is a bounded linear operator from a complete normed space to itself, it follows that
\beta
is a continuous operator and hence measurable. Therefore, the composite
\beta\mathcal{Z}
is a measurable operator from
(\Omega,\sigma(\mathcal{Z}))
to
(\mathbb{H},\mathscr{B}(\mathbb{H}))
. Moreover, we have
\mathbb{E}[\|\beta\mathcal{Z}\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{2}]\leq|\!|\!|\beta|\!|\!|^{2}\mathbb{E}[\|\mathcal{Z}\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{2}]<\infty
. Therefore, we have
V\subseteq L^{2}(\Omega,\sigma(\mathcal{Z}),\mu)\subset L^{2}(\Omega,\mathcal{F},\mu)
.

It is easy to see that
V
forms a linear subspace and hence what is left is to verify that
V
is closed. Let
\{\beta_{n}\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
be a sequence of indexed bounded linear operators
\beta_{n}\in\mathfrak{B}(\mathbb{H},\mathbb{H})
for
n\in\mathbb{N}
converging to
\beta
. Since
\mathfrak{B}(\mathbb{H},\mathbb{H})
is a Banach space, it follows that
\beta\in\mathfrak{B}(\mathbb{H},\mathbb{H})
. Therefore, we have
\{\beta_{n}\mathcal{Z}\}_{n\geq 1}
converging to
\beta\mathcal{Z}\in V
. Hence
V
is closed.

∎

Lemma A.6
.

Let
\mathcal{Z}_{1},\mathcal{Z}_{2}\in L^{2}(\Omega,\mathcal{F},\mu)
be centered Gaussian random elements taking values in
(\mathbb{H},\mathscr{B}(\mathbb{H}))
, where
\mathbb{H}
is a separable Hilbert space.
Let

\widehat{\beta}=\mathop{\mathrm{argmin}}_{\beta\in\mathfrak{B}(\mathbb{H},\mathbb{H})}\mathbb{E}\left[\|\mathcal{Z}_{1}-\beta\mathcal{Z}_{2}\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{2}\right].

Then, we have

\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{Z}_{1}\mid\mathcal{Z}_{2}]=\widehat{\beta}\mathcal{Z}_{2}.

This is a classical result when
\mathcal{Z}_{1},\mathcal{Z}_{2}
are Gaussian random variables taking values in the real line. The proof follows by the fact that uncorrelatedness implies independence under the jointly Gaussian setting. The extension to Gaussian random element in the Hilbert space is straightforward and yet we provide the statement for clarity.

Proof of Lemma
A.6
.

By Lemma
A.8
, we can write

\mathcal{Z}_{1}=e+\widehat{\beta}\mathcal{Z}_{2},

where we have
\mathbb{E}[\langle{e},{\widehat{\beta}\mathcal{Z}_{2}}\rangle_{\mathbb{H}}]=0
by the orthogonality principle of the Hilbert space. Since
{e}
and
{\widehat{\beta}\mathcal{Z}_{2}}
are uncorrelated, they are independent under the joint Gaussian assumption. Therefore, given
\mathcal{Z}_{2}=f
,
\widehat{\beta}\mathcal{Z}_{2}
is deterministic and the randomness
\mathcal{Z}_{2}\mid\mathcal{Z}_{1}
only comes from
e
. As a result,
\mathcal{Z}_{1}\mid\mathcal{Z}_{2}\sim\mathcal{N}(\widehat{\beta}\mathcal{Z}_{2},\mathscr{K}_{e})
, where
\mathscr{K}_{e}
is the covariance operator of
e
. Note that
\widehat{\beta}\mathcal{Z}_{2}
is the mean of
\mathcal{Z}_{1}\mid\mathcal{Z}_{2}
and hence we have
\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{Z}_{1}\mid\mathcal{Z}_{2}]=\widehat{\beta}\mathcal{Z}_{2}
.
∎

Lemma A.7
.

Let
\mathcal{Z}_{i}\in L^{2}(\Omega,\mathcal{F},\mu)
for
i=1,2,3
random elements taking values in
(\mathbb{H},\mathscr{B}(\mathbb{H}))
. We define
\mathcal{Z}_{1\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\bullet$}}}}3}
,
\mathcal{Z}_{2\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\bullet$}}}}3}
be the residuals of
\mathcal{Z}_{1}
and
\mathcal{Z}_{2}
after regressing on
\mathcal{Z}_{3}
, respectively, where recall the definition of regression in (
A.2
).
The mean elements of
\mathcal{Z}_{1\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\bullet$}}}}3}
,
\mathcal{Z}_{2\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\bullet$}}}}3}
are defined as
\mathfrak{m}_{1\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\bullet$}}}}3}
and
\mathfrak{m}_{2\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\bullet$}}}}3}
respectively.
Define the partial cross covariance operator

\langle{\mathscr{K}_{12\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\bullet$}}}}3}f},{g}\rangle=\mathbb{E}\left[\langle{(\mathcal{Z}_{1\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\bullet$}}}}3}-\mathfrak{m}_{1\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\bullet$}}}}3})},{g}\rangle_{\mathbb{H}}\langle{\mathcal{Z}_{2\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\bullet$}}}}3}-\mathfrak{m}_{2\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\bullet$}}}}3}},{f}\rangle_{\mathbb{H}}\right],\quad f,g\in\mathbb{H},

(A.5)

which is linear and bounded. Let
\mathscr{K}_{21\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\bullet$}}}}3}
be the adjoint operator of
\mathscr{K}_{12\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\bullet$}}}}3}
. Then,
\mathscr{K}_{21\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\bullet$}}}}3}
is linear and bounded.

If
\mathcal{Z}_{i}\in L^{2}(\Omega,\mathcal{F},\mu)
for
i=1,2,3
are zero-mean. Then the mean elements
\mathfrak{m}_{1\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\bullet$}}}}3}
and
\mathfrak{m}_{2\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\bullet$}}}}3}
are zero elements. Hence (
A.5
) is equivalent as (
A.3
).

Proof of Lemma
A.7
.

For notation simplicity, we define
\widetilde{\mathcal{Z}}_{i}=\mathcal{Z}_{i\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\bullet$}}}}3}-\mathfrak{m}_{i\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\bullet$}}}}3}
, the marginal probability measure of
\widetilde{\mathcal{Z}}_{i}
as
\widetilde{\mu}_{i}
for
i=1,2
and the joint probability measure of
\widetilde{\mathcal{Z}}_{1}
and
\widetilde{\mathcal{Z}}_{2}
as
\widetilde{\mu}_{12}
.
First, for any
f,g\in\mathbb{H}
, we define a bilinear functional
\widetilde{G}:\mathbb{H}\otimes\mathbb{H}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
:

\widetilde{G}(f,g)=\int_{\Omega\times\Omega}\langle{\widetilde{\mathcal{Z}}_{1}},{f}\rangle_{\mathbb{H}}\langle{\widetilde{\mathcal{Z}}_{2}},{g}\rangle_{\mathbb{H}}d\widetilde{\mu}_{12}.

We show that
\widetilde{G}(f,g)
is bounded for any bounded
f,g\in\mathbb{H}
by applying Cauchy-Schwarz inequality:

\displaystyle\left|\widetilde{G}(f,g)\right|^{2}

\displaystyle=\left(\int_{\Omega\times\Omega}\langle{\widetilde{\mathcal{Z}}_{1}},{f}\rangle_{\mathbb{H}}\langle{\widetilde{\mathcal{Z}}_{2}},{g}\rangle_{\mathbb{H}}d\widetilde{\mu}_{12}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\left\{\int_{\Omega}(\langle{\widetilde{\mathcal{Z}}_{1}},{f}\rangle_{\mathbb{H}})^{2}d\widetilde{\mu}_{1}\right\}\left\{\int_{\Omega}(\langle{\widetilde{\mathcal{Z}}_{2}},{g}\rangle_{\mathbb{H}})^{2}d\widetilde{\mu}_{2}\right\}.

Using the fact that
\mathcal{Z}_{i}=\widetilde{\mathcal{Z}}_{i}+(\mathcal{Z}_{i}-\widetilde{\mathcal{Z}}_{i})
and
E[\langle{\widetilde{\mathcal{Z}}_{i}},{\mathcal{Z}_{i}-\widetilde{\mathcal{Z}}_{i}}\rangle_{\mathbb{H}}]=0
for
i=1,2
, we could upper bound the above display as

\displaystyle\leq\left\{\int_{\Omega}(\langle{{\mathcal{Z}}_{1}},{f}\rangle_{\mathbb{H}})^{2}d{\mu}_{1}\right\}\left\{\int_{\Omega}(\langle{{\mathcal{Z}}_{2}},{g}\rangle_{\mathbb{H}})^{2}d{\mu}_{2}\right\}

\displaystyle=\|\mathscr{K}_{1}^{1/2}f\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{2}\|\mathscr{K}_{2}^{1/2}g\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{2}<\infty.

(A.6)

Therefore, for any fixed
g\in\mathbb{H}
, by Riesz’ representation theorem, there exists a unique element
q_{g}
in
\mathbb{H}
such that
G(f,g)=\langle{f},{q_{g}}\rangle
. Then, we define
\mathscr{K}_{12\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\bullet$}}}}3}:\mathbb{H}\rightarrow\mathbb{H}
by
\mathscr{K}_{12\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\bullet$}}}}3}g=q_{g}
. It is clear that
\mathscr{K}_{12\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\bullet$}}}}3}
is linear and bounded by (
A.6
):

\displaystyle\|\mathscr{K}_{12\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\bullet$}}}}3}g\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{2}=\sup_{f\in\mathbb{H}}\frac{|\widetilde{G}(f,g)|^{2}}{\|f\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{2}}\leq\sup_{f\in\mathbb{H}}\frac{\|\mathscr{K}_{1}^{1/2}f\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{2}}{\|f\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{2}}\|\mathscr{K}_{2}^{1/2}g\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{2}\leq\|\mathscr{K}_{1}\|\|\mathscr{K}_{2}\|\|g\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{2},

which implies that
\|\mathscr{K}_{12\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\bullet$}}}}3}\|\leq\|\mathscr{K}_{1}\|\|\mathscr{K}_{2}\|<\infty
.
\mathscr{K}_{12\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\bullet$}}}}3}
is defined everywhere in
\mathbb{H}
.
∎

Lemma A.8
.

Under the same condition of Lemma
A.7
, define covariance operator as
\mathscr{K}_{12}
. Define

\widehat{\beta}=\mathop{\mathrm{argmin}}_{\begin{subarray}{c}\beta\in\mathfrak{B}(\mathbb{H},\mathbb{H}).\end{subarray}}\mathbb{E}\left[\|\mathcal{Z}_{1}-\beta\mathcal{Z}_{2}\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{2}\right].

Then,
\widehat{\beta}
satisfies
\mathscr{K}_{12}=\widehat{\beta}\mathscr{K}_{2}
.

since
\Image(\mathscr{K}_{21})\subseteq\Image(\mathscr{K}_{2})
, it follows that
\widehat{\beta}=(\mathscr{K}_{2}^{\dagger}\mathscr{K}_{21})^{*}
.

Proof of Lemma
A.8
.

Define the space

V=\{\beta\mathcal{Z}_{2}:\beta\in\mathfrak{B}(\mathbb{H},\mathbb{H})\}
. By Lemma
A.5
, we see that
V
is a closed subspace of
L^{2}(\Omega,\mathcal{F},\mu)
.

Then, by the orthogonality principle of the Hilbert space (see
Conway 2007
for an exposition), we have
\mathbb{E}[(\mathcal{Z}_{1}-\widehat{\beta}\mathcal{Z}_{2})\otimes\mathcal{Z}_{2}]=0
. This implies that for any
f,g\in\mathbb{H}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\langle{\mathcal{Z}_{1}-\widehat{\beta}\mathcal{Z}_{2}},{f}\rangle_{\mathbb{H}}\langle{\mathcal{Z}_{2}},{g}\rangle_{\mathbb{H}}]

\displaystyle=\langle{(\mathscr{K}_{12}-\widehat{\beta}\mathscr{K}_{2})g},{f}\rangle_{\mathbb{H}}=0.

Therefore, we can conclude that
\mathscr{K}_{12}-\widehat{\beta}\mathscr{K}_{2}=0
.
∎

Appendix B
Truncation in Finite Dimensional Space

In this section, we first show the proof of Proposition
3.2
, which transforms the optimization problem (
3.3
) in the Hilbert space into an optimization problem of sequences of real numbers. Then, in Appendix
B.2
, we derive and propose a finite dimensional optimization problem that computing resources can realize.

B.1
Proof of Proposition
3.2

Recall that for
m=1,\ldots,M
and
i\in V
we have

{\mathscr{A}^{m}}=\sum_{\ell,\ell^{\prime\prime}\in\mathbb{N}}a_{\ell\ell^{\prime\prime}}^{m}\phi^{m}_{\ell}\otimes\phi_{\ell^{\prime\prime}}^{m},\quad\beta_{ij}=\sum_{\ell,\ell^{\prime}\in\mathbb{N}}b_{ij,\ell\ell^{\prime}}\phi_{\ell}\otimes\phi_{\ell^{\prime}},\quad{\mathcal{Y}_{i}^{m}}=\sum_{\ell^{\prime\prime}\in\mathbb{N}}y_{i,\ell^{\prime\prime}}^{m}\phi_{\ell^{\prime\prime}}^{m}.

Therefore, we can write

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\bigg\|{\mathscr{A}^{m}}{\mathcal{Y}_{i}^{m}}-\sum_{j\in{V\backslash\{i\}}}\beta_{ij}{\mathscr{A}^{m}}\mathcal{Y}_{j}^{m}\bigg\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\quad=\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\Bigg\|\left(\sum_{\ell,\ell^{\prime\prime}\in\mathbb{N}}a_{\ell\ell^{\prime\prime}}^{m}\phi^{m}_{\ell}\otimes\phi_{\ell^{\prime\prime}}^{m}\right)\left(\sum_{\ell^{\prime\prime}\in\mathbb{N}}y_{i,\ell^{\prime\prime}}^{m}\phi_{\ell^{\prime\prime}}^{m}\right)

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad-\sum_{j\in{V\backslash\{i\}}}\left(\sum_{\ell,\ell^{\prime}\in\mathbb{N}}b_{ij,\ell\ell^{\prime}}\phi_{\ell}\otimes\phi_{\ell^{\prime}}\right)\left(\sum_{\ell^{\prime},\ell^{\prime\prime}\in\mathbb{N}}a_{\ell^{\prime}\ell^{\prime\prime}}^{m}\phi^{m}_{\ell^{\prime}}\otimes\phi_{\ell^{\prime\prime}}^{m}\right)\left(\sum_{\ell^{\prime\prime}\in\mathbb{N}}y_{j,\ell^{\prime\prime}}^{m}\phi_{\ell^{\prime\prime}}^{m}\right)\Bigg\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{2}\Bigg]

\displaystyle\quad=\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\Bigg\|\left(\sum_{\ell,\ell^{\prime\prime}\in\mathbb{N}}a_{\ell\ell^{\prime\prime}}^{m}y_{i,\ell^{\prime\prime}}^{m}\phi_{\ell}\right)-\sum_{j\in{V\backslash\{i\}}}\left(\sum_{\ell,\ell^{\prime},\ell^{\prime\prime}\in\mathbb{N}}b_{ij,\ell\ell^{\prime}}a_{\ell^{\prime}\ell^{\prime\prime}}^{m}y_{j,\ell^{\prime\prime}}^{m}\phi_{\ell}\right)\Bigg\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{2}\Bigg]

\displaystyle\quad=\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{\ell\in\mathbb{N}}\left(\sum_{\ell^{\prime\prime}\in\mathbb{N}}a_{\ell\ell^{\prime\prime}}^{m}y_{i,\ell^{\prime\prime}}^{m}-\sum_{j\in{V\backslash\{i\}}}\sum_{\ell^{\prime},\ell^{\prime\prime}\in\mathbb{N}}b_{ij,\ell\ell^{\prime}}a_{\ell^{\prime}\ell^{\prime\prime}}^{m}y_{j,\ell^{\prime\prime}}^{m}\right)^{2}\right].

Hence we complete the proof.

B.2
Derivation of Equation (
3.6
)

Similar to the latent space, we define the
k_{m}
-dimensional approximation of
\chi_{i}^{m}
as

\chi_{i}^{k_{m}}:=\sum_{\ell=1}^{k_{m}}x_{i,\ell}^{m}\phi_{\ell}^{m},\quad i\in V,\;m=1,\ldots,M,

where
x_{i,\ell}^{m}=\langle{\chi_{i}^{m}},{\phi_{\ell}^{m}}\rangle
for
\ell=1,\ldots,k_{m}
.
Then, we can represent the truncated
\mathbf{z}_{i}=(z_{i,1},\ldots,z_{i,k})^{\top}\in\mathbb{R}^{k}
from the infinite dimensional vector
(z_{i,1},z_{i,2},\ldots)
in the following form

\displaystyle\mathbf{z}_{i}

\displaystyle=\begin{pmatrix}z_{i,1}\\
\vdots\\
z_{i,k}\end{pmatrix}=\sum_{j\in[p]\backslash\{i\}}\begin{pmatrix}b_{ij,11}^{\star}&\ldots&b_{ij,1k}^{\star}\\
&\ddots&\\
b_{ij,k1}^{\star}&\ldots&b_{ij,kk}^{\star}\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}z_{j,1}\\
\vdots\\
z_{j,k}\end{pmatrix}+\mathbf{r}_{i}+\mathbf{w}_{i}=\sum_{j\in[p]\backslash\{i\}}\mathbf{B}_{ij}^{\star}\mathbf{z}_{j}+\mathbf{r}_{i}+\mathbf{w}_{i},

(B.1)

where

r_{i,\ell}=\sum_{j\in{V\backslash\{i\}}}\sum_{\ell^{\prime}=k+1}^{\infty}\langle{b_{ij,\ell\ell^{\prime}}^{\star}\phi_{\ell^{\prime}}},{\mathcal{Z}_{j}}\rangle

,
w_{i,\ell}=\langle{\mathcal{W}_{i}},{\phi_{\ell}}\rangle
for
\ell=1,\ldots,k

and
\mathbf{r}_{i}=(r_{i,1},\ldots,r_{i,k})^{\top}\in\mathbb{R}^{k}
denotes the residual vector. Let
\mathbb{E}(\mathbf{r}_{i}\mathbf{r}_{i}^{\top})=\bm{\Sigma}_{i}^{\mathbf{r}}
and
\mathbb{E}(\mathbf{w}_{i}\mathbf{w}_{i}^{\top})=\bm{\Sigma}_{i}^{\mathbf{w}}
for
i\in V
.

Following (
3.1
), we can also express (
B.1
) as

\displaystyle\mathcal{Z}^{k}_{i}=\sum_{\ell=1}^{k}\langle{{\mathscr{A}^{m}}(\chi_{i}^{m})},{\phi_{\ell}}\rangle\phi_{\ell}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\ell^{\prime}=1}^{\infty}\sum_{\ell=1}^{k}\langle{a_{\ell\ell^{\prime}}^{m\star}\phi_{\ell^{\prime}}^{m}},{\chi_{i}^{m}}\rangle\phi_{\ell}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\ell=1}^{k}\left(\sum_{\ell^{\prime}=1}^{k_{m}}\langle{a_{\ell\ell^{\prime}}^{m\star}\phi_{\ell^{\prime}}^{m}},{\chi_{i}^{k_{m}}}\rangle\phi_{\ell}+\sum_{\ell^{\prime}=k_{m}+1}^{\infty}\langle{a_{\ell\ell^{\prime}}^{m\star}\phi_{\ell^{\prime}}^{m}},{\chi_{i}^{m}}\rangle\phi_{\ell}\right).

(B.2)

To simplify the analysis, we assume that the second term is
0
.
Then, Equation (
B.2
) has the following equivalent expression in matrix form

\mathbf{z}_{i}=\begin{pmatrix}z_{i,1}\\
\vdots\\
z_{i,k}\end{pmatrix}=\begin{pmatrix}a_{11}^{m\star}&\ldots&a_{1k_{m}}^{m\star}\\
&\ddots&\\
a_{k_{m}1}^{m\star}&\ldots&a_{kk_{m}}^{m\star}\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}x_{i,1}^{m}\\
\vdots\\
x_{i,k_{m}}^{m}\end{pmatrix}+\begin{pmatrix}u_{i,1}^{m}\\
\vdots\\
u_{i,k}^{m}\end{pmatrix}=\mathbf{A}^{m\star}\mathbf{x}_{i}^{m}+\mathbf{u}_{i}^{m},

(B.3)

where

u_{i,\ell}^{m}=\sum_{\ell^{\prime}=k+1}^{\infty}\langle{a_{\ell\ell^{\prime}}^{m\star}\phi_{\ell^{\prime}}^{m}},{\chi_{i}^{m}}\rangle
for
\ell=1,\ldots,k

and
\mathbf{u}_{i}^{m}=(u_{i,1}^{m},\ldots,u_{i,k}^{m})^{\top}\in\mathbb{R}^{k}
is the truncation error in the observation space.
Let
\mathbf{u}^{m}=(\mathbf{u}_{1}^{m\top},\ldots,\mathbf{u}_{p}^{m\top})^{\top}\in\mathbb{R}^{kp}
and
\mathbb{E}(\mathbf{u}^{m}\mathbf{u}^{m\top})=\bm{\Sigma}^{m,\mathbf{u}}
. Combining (
B.1
) and (
B.3
) together, we then have the relationship

\mathbf{A}^{m\star}\mathbf{x}_{i}^{m}+\mathbf{u}_{i}^{m}-\left\{\sum_{j\in{V\backslash\{i\}}}\mathbf{B}_{ij}^{\star}\left(\mathbf{A}^{m\star}\mathbf{x}_{j}^{m}+\mathbf{u}_{j}^{m}\right)+\mathbf{r}_{i}+\mathbf{w}_{i}\right\}={\bf 0},

(B.4)

where
\mathbf{w}_{i}
follows independently from
\mathcal{N}({\bf 0},\bm{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{w}}_{i})
. Let
{\mathbf{q}}_{i}^{m}=(q_{i,1}^{m},\ldots,q_{i,k_{m}}^{m})^{\top}\in\mathbb{R}^{k_{m}}
where
q_{i,j}^{m}=\langle{\xi_{i}^{m}},{\phi_{j}^{m}}\rangle
for
j=1,\ldots k_{m}
, we define the finite truncation of the observed random vector as

\mathbf{y}_{i}^{m}=\mathbf{x}_{i}^{m}+\mathbf{q}_{i}^{m},\qquad m=1,\ldots,M,i\in V,

(B.5)

where
\mathbf{q}_{i}^{m}
is the noise in the observation space and follows the distribution
\mathcal{N}({\bf 0},\bm{\Sigma}^{m,\mathbf{q}}_{i})
. Therefore, under the assumption that
k_{m}
is large enough such that
\mathbf{q}_{i}^{m}
are small in magnitude, we can approximate (
B.4
) as

\mathbf{A}^{m\star}\mathbf{y}_{i}^{m}+\mathbf{u}_{i}^{m}-\left\{\sum_{j\in{V\backslash\{i\}}}\mathbf{B}_{ij}^{\star}\left(\mathbf{A}^{m\star}\mathbf{y}_{j}^{m}+\mathbf{u}_{j}^{m}\right)+\mathbf{r}_{i}+\mathbf{w}_{i}\right\}\approx{\bf 0}.

Therefore, a reasonable choice of the finite dimensional realization of the original programming (
3.5
) is

\min_{\{{\mathbf{A}^{m}}\},\{\mathbf{B}_{ij}\}}\sum_{m=1}^{M}\sum_{i=1}^{p}\mathbb{E}\left[\bigg\|{\mathbf{A}^{m}}\mathbf{y}_{i}^{m}-\sum_{j\in{V\backslash\{i\}}}\mathbf{B}_{ij}{\mathbf{A}^{m}}\mathbf{y}_{j}^{m}\bigg\|_{F}^{2}\right].

Appendix C
Notations

We define the following quantities

\displaystyle\Upsilon_{1}=\frac{3\nu_{x}\nu_{b}\rho_{x}\rho_{b}}{16},\quad\Upsilon_{2}=\frac{\nu_{x}\nu_{b}+3\rho_{x}\rho_{b}}{4};

\displaystyle\Upsilon_{3}=\|\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}^{m}\|_{2}(C_{B}+1)\max_{m=1,\ldots,M}\|\mathbf{A}^{m(0)}\|_{2}\max_{i\in V}(\|\mathbf{B}_{i}^{\star}\|_{F}\vee\|\widetilde{\mathbf{B}}_{i}^{\star}\|_{1});

\displaystyle\Upsilon_{4}=2\sqrt{(1+\vartheta_{1})(1+\vartheta_{2})\tau\alpha^{\star}},\quad\Upsilon_{5}=4(1+C_{A})^{2}(2+C_{B})^{2}\rho_{b}\rho_{a}\max_{m=1,\ldots,M}\|\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}^{m}\|_{2}^{2};

\displaystyle\Upsilon_{6}=\frac{\nu_{x}\nu_{a}}{2},\quad\Upsilon_{7}=\rho_{a}^{2}\max_{m=1,\ldots,M}\|\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}^{m}\|_{2}^{2};

\displaystyle\Upsilon_{8}=C_{0}\rho_{x}\rho_{b}\left\{\rho_{b}\left(\max_{m=1,\ldots,M}\|\bm{\Sigma}^{m,\mathbf{u}}\|_{2}+\rho_{a}\|\bm{\Sigma}^{m,\mathbf{q}}\|\right)+\max_{i\in V}\left(\|\bm{\Sigma}_{i}^{\mathbf{r}}\|_{2}+\|\bm{\Sigma}_{i}^{\mathbf{w}}\|_{2}\right)\right\};

\displaystyle\Upsilon_{9}=C_{1}C_{\vartheta}(1+\vartheta_{1})\rho_{x}\rho_{a}\left\{\rho_{b}\left(\max_{m=1,\ldots,M}\|\bm{\Sigma}^{m,\mathbf{u}}\|_{2}+\rho_{a}\|\bm{\Sigma}^{m,\mathbf{q}}\|\right)+\max_{i\in V}\left(\|\bm{\Sigma}_{i}^{\mathbf{r}}\|_{2}+\|\bm{\Sigma}_{i}^{\mathbf{w}}\|_{2}\right)\right\};

\displaystyle C_{\vartheta}=\left(1+\sqrt{\frac{2}{\vartheta_{1}-2}}\right)^{2}\left(1+\sqrt{\frac{4}{\vartheta_{2}-2}}\right)^{2},\quad C_{\alpha}=\frac{\Upsilon_{8}}{\Upsilon_{1}},\quad C_{\beta}=\frac{\Upsilon_{9}}{\Upsilon_{7}};

\displaystyle C_{\gamma}=\max_{m=1,\ldots,M}\rho_{b}^{1/2}\|\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}^{m}\|_{2}\left\{\sigma_{\max}({\mathbf{A}^{m}}^{(0)})+\rho_{a}^{1/2}\right\}\sqrt{(1+\vartheta_{1})s^{\star}};

\displaystyle C_{\delta,i}=C_{2}\sqrt{(1+\vartheta_{2})(1+\vartheta_{1})}\max_{m=1,\ldots,M}\rho_{x}^{1/2}\rho_{a}^{1/2}\{\rho_{b}^{1/2}\|\bm{\Sigma}^{m,\mathbf{u}}\|_{2}^{1/2}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+(\rho_{a}\rho_{b})^{1/2}\|\bm{\Sigma}^{m,\mathbf{q}}\|_{2}^{1/2}+\|\bm{\Sigma}_{i}^{\mathbf{r}}\|_{2}^{1/2}+\|\bm{\Sigma}_{i}^{\mathbf{w}}\|_{2}^{1/2}\};

\displaystyle C_{\gamma_{k},\nu,\rho}=C_{3}\bigg\{\gamma_{k}^{-1/2}(\rho_{x}^{1/2}\nu_{x}^{-2}+\nu_{x}^{-3/2})+\rho_{x}^{2}\nu_{x}^{-5/2}(\gamma_{k}^{-1/2}+\gamma_{k}^{-2})(\rho_{x}^{1/2}\nu_{x}^{-1/2}+1)\bigg\}

\displaystyle C_{\eta_{A}}=\frac{3\Upsilon_{1}}{4\Upsilon_{2}},\quad C_{\eta_{B}}=\frac{\Upsilon_{6}}{4},

where
C_{0},C_{1},C_{2},C_{3},C_{4},C_{5}
are absolute constants,
which
are independent of data parameters:
M,N,p,s^{\star},\alpha
,
upper and lower bounds of the singular values:
\nu_{x},\rho_{x},\nu_{b},\rho_{b},\nu_{a},\rho_{a}
, model parameters:
k,k_{m},C_{A},C_{B}
, and tuning parameters:
\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2},\tau_{1},\tau_{2}
.

Appendix D
Analysis of Edge Estimation

The section discusses the analysis of edge estimation, where the results are presented in Theorem
5.3
–
5.4
. We begin with introducing some notations and math formulations that will assist the analysis in Section
D.1
. In Section
D.2
, we discuss the shrinkage of the distance metric
\max_{m=1,\ldots,M}\|{\mathbf{A}^{m}}-\mathbf{A}^{m\star}\|_{F}^{2}
and
\max_{i\in V}\|\mathbf{B}_{i}-\mathbf{B}_{i}^{\star}\|_{F}^{2}
per iterate of Algorithm
1
along with the statistical error induced by the finite sample size setting. In section
D.3
, we combine the results from Section
D.2
to state the contraction of the distance metric
\max_{m=1,\ldots,M}\|{\mathbf{A}^{m}}-\mathbf{A}^{m\star}\|_{F}^{2}+\max_{i\in V}\|\mathbf{B}_{i}-\mathbf{B}_{i}^{\star}\|_{F}^{2}
per iterate of Algorithm
1
and the underlying conditions. With results from Section
D.3
in hand, we are able to apply the telescoping technique to show convergence of Algorithm
1
. The analysis is presented in Section
D.4
. Section
D.5
–
D.6
present the proof of lemmas used in Section
D.2
–
D.4
.

D.1
Notations

For analysis simplicity, we can write the objective function (
3.7
) as

\displaystyle f(\mathbf{A},\mathbf{B})

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{p}\sum_{m=1}^{M}\frac{1}{2N}\bigg\|{\mathbf{A}^{m}}{\mathbf{Y}_{i}^{m}}-\sum_{j\in{V\backslash\{i\}}}\mathbf{B}_{ij}{\mathbf{A}^{m}}{\mathbf{Y}_{j}^{m}}\bigg\|_{F}^{2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{p}\sum_{m=1}^{M}\frac{1}{2N}\left\|{\mathbf{A}^{m}}{\mathbf{Y}_{i}^{m}}-\mathbf{B}_{i}(\mathbf{I}_{p-1}\otimes{\mathbf{A}^{m}}){\mathbf{Y}_{\backslash\{i\}}^{m}}\right\|_{F}^{2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{p}\sum_{m=1}^{M}\frac{1}{2N}\|\widetilde{\mathbf{B}}_{i}(\mathbf{I}_{p}\otimes{\mathbf{A}^{m}})\mathbf{Y}^{m}\|_{F}^{2},

(D.1)

where
\otimes
denote the Kronecker product. Define

^

Σ

^m=N^-1
Y
^m(
Y
^m)^⊤,
^

Σ

^m_⋅ \{i}=N^-1
Y
^m(
Y
_\{i}^m)^⊤.

Then, for any matrix
\Delta\in\mathbb{R}^{k\times k_{m}}
, we can write

\langle{\nabla_{{\mathbf{A}^{m}}}f(\mathbf{A},\mathbf{B})},{\Delta}\rangle=\sum_{i=1}^{p}\langle{\widetilde{\mathbf{B}}_{i}^{\top}\widetilde{\mathbf{B}}_{i}(\mathbf{I}_{p}\otimes{\mathbf{A}^{m}})\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}^{m}},{(\mathbf{I}_{p}\otimes\Delta)}\rangle

(D.2)

for
m=1,\ldots,M
. Moreover, the gradient of
f(\mathbf{A},\mathbf{B})
with respect to
{\mathbf{A}^{m}}
for
m=1,\ldots,M
is

\nabla_{{\mathbf{A}^{m}}}f(\mathbf{A},\mathbf{B})=\sum_{j=1}^{p}(\mathbf{e}_{j}^{\top}\otimes\mathbf{I}_{k})\left\{\sum_{i=1}^{p}\widetilde{\mathbf{B}}_{i}^{\top}\widetilde{\mathbf{B}}_{i}(\mathbf{I}_{p}\otimes{\mathbf{A}^{m}})\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}^{m}\right\}(\mathbf{e}_{j}\otimes\mathbf{I}_{k_{m}}).

(D.3)

Similarly, for any matrix
\Delta\in\mathbb{R}^{k\times k(p-1)}
, we can write

\langle{\nabla_{\mathbf{B}_{i}}f(\mathbf{A},\mathbf{B})},{\Delta}\rangle=\sum_{m=1}^{M}\langle{-\widetilde{\mathbf{B}}_{i}(\mathbf{I}_{p}\otimes{\mathbf{A}^{m}})\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}^{m}_{\cdot\backslash\{i\}}(\mathbf{I}_{p-1}\otimes{\mathbf{A}^{m}})^{\top}},{\Delta}\rangle

(D.4)

for every
i\in V
and
j\in{V\backslash\{i\}}
.

D.2
Preliminaries: One-Step Update and statistical quantities

We show Theorem
5.3
in several steps. We begin with showing the contraction of distance metric per iterate of Algorithm
1
.
First, given the current estimate
{\mathbf{A}^{m}}
for
m=1,\ldots,M
, we define an update of
{\mathbf{A}^{m}}
from Algorithm
1
as

\displaystyle\mathbf{A}^{m+}=\mathcal{P}_{\tau}({\mathbf{A}^{m}}-\eta_{A}\nabla_{{\mathbf{A}^{m}}}f(\mathbf{A},\mathbf{B})),\quad m=1,\ldots,M;

Similarly, for every
i\in V
, we define the update of
\mathbf{B}_{i}
as
\mathbf{B}_{i}^{+}
. Let

\displaystyle{\mathcal{S}}_{i}^{\star}=\text{supp}(\mathbf{B}_{i}^{\star}),\quad{\mathcal{S}}_{i}=\text{supp}(\mathbf{B}_{i}),\quad{\mathcal{S}}^{+}_{i}=\text{supp}(\mathbf{B}_{i}^{+}),\quad\bar{\mathcal{S}}_{i}={\mathcal{S}}^{\star}_{i}\cup{\mathcal{S}}_{i}\cup{\mathcal{S}}_{i}^{+}.

(D.5)

By definition of
\mathbf{B}_{i}^{\star}
, we have
\left|{\mathcal{S}}_{i}^{\star}\right|\leq s^{\star}(\alpha^{\star}k)^{2}
and
\left|{\mathcal{S}}_{i}\right|,\left|{\mathcal{S}}_{i}^{+}\right|\leq s(\alpha k)^{2}=(\vartheta_{1}s^{\star})(\vartheta_{2}\alpha^{\star}k)^{2}/8
. Taking the union bound, it follows that
\left|\bar{\mathcal{S}}_{i}\right|\leq(1+\vartheta_{1})s^{\star}(1+\vartheta_{2})^{2}(\alpha^{\star}k)^{2}
.

Similarly, we define the corresponding sets of node indices as

\displaystyle\mathscr{N}_{i}^{\star}=\{j:\|\mathbf{B}_{ij}^{\star}\|_{F}>\epsilon_{0}\},\quad\mathscr{N}_{i}=\{j:\|\mathbf{B}_{ij}\|_{F}>\epsilon_{0}\};\;

\displaystyle\mathscr{N}_{i}^{+}=\{j:\|\mathbf{B}_{i,j}^{+}\|_{F}>\epsilon_{0}\},\quad\bar{\mathscr{N}}_{i}=\mathscr{N}_{i}^{\star}\cup\mathscr{N}_{i}\cup\mathscr{N}_{i}^{+},

(D.6)

with
|\bar{\mathscr{N}}_{i}|\leq(1+\vartheta_{1})s^{\star}
for
i\in V
.

By definition, we have

\displaystyle\mathbf{B}_{i}^{+}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{H}_{\alpha}\circ{\mathcal{T}}_{s}\left(\mathbf{B}_{i}-\eta_{B}\nabla_{\mathbf{B}_{i}}f(\mathbf{A},\mathbf{B})\right)

\displaystyle=\mathcal{H}_{\alpha}\circ{\mathcal{T}}_{s}\left(\mathbf{B}_{i}-\eta_{B}[\nabla_{\mathbf{B}_{i}}f(\mathbf{A},\mathbf{B})]_{\bar{\mathcal{S}}_{i}}\right),

(D.7)

for
i\in V
.

With definitions of
{\mathbf{A}^{m}}^{+}
for
m=1,\ldots,M
and
\mathbf{B}_{i}^{+}
for
i\in V
, Lemma
D.1
states the contraction of the distance
\|{\mathbf{A}^{m}}^{+}-\mathbf{A}^{m\star}\|_{F}^{2}
with respect to
\|{\mathbf{A}^{m}}-\mathbf{A}^{m\star}\|_{F}^{2}
for
m=1,\ldots,M
while Lemma
D.2
states the contraction of
\|\mathbf{B}_{i}^{+}-\mathbf{B}_{i}^{\star}\|_{F}^{2}
with respect to
\|\mathbf{B}_{i}-\mathbf{B}_{i}^{\star}\|_{F}^{2}
for
i\in V
per iterate of Algorithm
1
. We leave the details of the proof in Section
D.5
.

Lemma D.1

(One-Step Update of
{\mathbf{A}^{m}}
)
.

Recall that
\Upsilon_{j}
for
j=1,\ldots,6
are constants defined in Section
C
.
Suppose that
k\leq\min_{m=1,\ldots,M}k_{m}
and Assumption
6
–
7
hold.
There exists constants
C_{A}
and
C_{B}
such that
\|{\mathbf{A}^{m}}-\mathbf{A}^{m\star}\|_{2}\leq C_{A}\|\mathbf{A}^{m\star}\|_{2}
,
\|\mathbf{B}_{i}-\mathbf{B}_{i}^{\star}\|\leq C_{B}\|\mathbf{B}_{i}^{\star}\|
for any unitarily invariant norm and
\|\mathbf{B}_{i}^{\top}-\mathbf{B}_{i}^{\star\top}\|_{1}\leq C_{B}\|\mathbf{B}_{i}^{\star\top}\|_{1}

for
m=1,\ldots,M,\;i\in V
.
Let
\kappa_{m}=\sigma_{\max}(\bm{\Sigma}^{m})/\sigma_{\min}(\bm{\Sigma}^{m})
,
N=O(\kappa_{m}^{2}(\max_{m=1,\ldots,M}k_{m}s^{\star}+\log p+\log M))
and
\eta_{A}\leq{2}/\{3p(s^{\star}+1)\Upsilon_{2}\}
, then

\displaystyle\max_{m=1,\ldots,M}\|\mathbf{A}^{m+}-\mathbf{A}^{m\star}\|_{F}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq 4\bigg[\left\{1-\eta_{A}\frac{3p(s^{\star}+1)\Upsilon_{1}}{2\Upsilon_{2}}+\eta_{A}\frac{2ps^{\star}\Upsilon_{4}^{2}\Upsilon_{3}^{2}}{\Upsilon_{6}}\right\}\max_{m=1,\ldots,M}\|{\mathbf{A}^{m}}-\mathbf{A}^{m\star}\|_{F}^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+k\eta_{A}\left\{\frac{4\Upsilon_{2}}{p(s^{\star}+1)\Upsilon_{1}}+3\eta_{A}\right\}\max_{m=1,\ldots,M}\|\nabla_{{\mathbf{A}^{m}}}f(\mathbf{A}^{\star},\mathbf{B}^{\star})\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+p\eta_{A}\left\{\frac{\Upsilon_{6}}{2}+3p(s^{\star}+1)\Upsilon_{5}\eta_{A}\right\}\max_{i\in V}\|\mathbf{B}_{i}-\mathbf{B}_{i}^{\star}\|_{F}^{2}\bigg],

(D.8)

with probability at least
1-\max_{m=1,\ldots,M}6\exp\{-k_{m}(s^{\star}+1)\}
.

Lemma D.2

(One-Step Update of
\mathbf{B}_{ij}
)
.

Recall that
\Upsilon_{j}
for
j=3,\ldots,7
are constants defined in Section
C
.
Suppose that
k\leq\min_{m=1,\ldots,M}k_{m}
and Assumption
3
–
4
,
6
–
7
hold.
There exists constants
C_{A}
and
C_{B}
such that
\|{\mathbf{A}^{m}}-\mathbf{A}^{m\star}\|_{2}\leq C_{A}\|\mathbf{A}^{m\star}\|_{2}
,
\|\mathbf{B}_{i}-\mathbf{B}_{i}^{\star}\|\leq C_{B}\|\mathbf{B}_{i}^{\star}\|
for any unitarily invariant norm and
\|\mathbf{B}_{i}^{\top}-\mathbf{B}_{i}^{\star\top}\|_{1}\leq C_{B}\|\mathbf{B}_{i}^{\star\top}\|_{1}

for
m=1,\ldots,M,\;i\in V
.
Let
\kappa_{m}=\sigma_{\max}(\bm{\Sigma}^{m})/\sigma_{\min}(\bm{\Sigma}^{m})
and
N=O(\kappa_{m}^{2}(\max_{m=1,\ldots,M}k_{m}s^{\star}+\log p+\log M))
.
Then, we have

\displaystyle\max_{i\in V}\|\mathbf{B}_{i}^{+}-\mathbf{B}_{i}^{\star}\|_{F}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C_{\vartheta}\bigg\{\left(1-\eta_{B}M\Upsilon_{6}+3\eta_{B}^{2}M^{2}\Upsilon_{7}\right)\max_{i\in V}\|\mathbf{B}_{i}-\mathbf{B}_{i}^{\star}\|_{F}^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+Ms^{\star}\eta_{B}\left(\frac{2\Upsilon_{4}^{2}\Upsilon_{3}^{2}}{\Upsilon_{6}}+3\Upsilon_{5}M\eta_{B}\right)\max_{m=1,\ldots,M}\|{\mathbf{A}^{m}}-\mathbf{A}^{m\star}\|_{F}^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+(1+\vartheta_{1})s^{\star}\eta_{B}\left(\frac{2}{M\Upsilon_{6}}+3\eta_{B}\right)\max_{i\in V}\|[\nabla_{\mathbf{B}_{i}}f(\mathbf{A}^{\star},\mathbf{B}^{\star})]_{\bar{\mathcal{S}}_{i}}\|^{2}_{r(k,\infty)}\bigg\},

(D.9)

with probability at least
1-\max_{m=1,\ldots,M}\exp(-k_{m}s^{\star})
.

Observe that the right hand sides of (
D.8
) and (
D.9
) has the term of
\|\nabla_{{\mathbf{A}^{m}}}f(\mathbf{A}^{\star},\mathbf{B}^{\star})\|_{2}^{2}
and
\|[\nabla_{\mathbf{B}_{i}}f(\mathbf{A}^{\star},\mathbf{B}^{\star})]_{\bar{\mathcal{S}}_{i}}\|^{2}_{r(k,\infty)}
, respectively. By law of large number, ideally, these quantities would be negligible as the number of sample size goes to infinity. Specifically,
(\mathbf{A}^{\star},\mathbf{B}^{\star})
is a stationary point of
\mathbb{E}f(\mathbf{A}^{\star},\mathbf{B}^{\star})
. Hence, we expect that
\|\nabla_{{\mathbf{A}^{m}}}f(\mathbf{A}^{\star},\mathbf{B}^{\star})\|_{2}^{2}
and
\|[\nabla_{\mathbf{B}_{i}}f(\mathbf{A}^{\star},\mathbf{B}^{\star})]_{\bar{\mathcal{S}}_{i}}\|^{2}_{r(k,\infty)}
to be small when we have some reasonable number of sample size. Lemma
D.3
states the upper bound of the
\|\nabla_{{\mathbf{A}^{m}}}f(\mathbf{A}^{\star},\mathbf{B}^{\star})\|_{2}
for
m=1,\ldots,M
while Lemma
D.4
provides the upper bound of
\|[\nabla_{\mathbf{B}_{i}}f(\mathbf{A}^{\star},\mathbf{B}^{\star})]_{\bar{\mathcal{S}}_{i}}\|^{2}_{r(k,\infty)}
for
i\in V
.

Lemma D.3

(statistical error bound of
\nabla_{{\mathbf{A}^{m}}}f(\mathbf{A}^{\star},\mathbf{B}^{\star})
)
.

Under conditions of Lemma
D.1
and assume that
N=O(C_{\epsilon}^{2}(k+k_{m}+\log M))
. Let
C_{\epsilon}=C_{3}\max_{i\in V}\rho_{x}^{1/2}\rho_{b}^{1/2}\big\{\rho_{b}^{1/2}\|\bm{\Sigma}^{m,\mathbf{u}}\|_{2}^{1/2}+(\rho_{b}\rho_{a})^{1/2}\|\bm{\Sigma}^{m,\mathbf{q}}\|_{2}^{1/2}+\|\bm{\Sigma}_{i}^{\mathbf{r}}\|_{2}^{1/2}+\|\bm{\Sigma}_{i}^{\mathbf{w}}\|_{2}^{1/2}\big\}
for some absolute constant
C_{3}>0
.

Then, we have

\frac{1}{p(s^{\star}+1)}\|\nabla_{{\mathbf{A}^{m}}}f(\mathbf{A}^{\star},\mathbf{B}^{\star})\|_{2}>C_{\epsilon}\sqrt{\frac{k_{m}+k+\log M}{N}},

with probability smaller than
M^{-1}\exp\{-(k_{m}+k)\}
.

Lemma D.4

(statistical error bound of
\nabla_{\mathbf{B}_{i}}f(\mathbf{A}^{\star},\mathbf{B}^{\star})
)
.

Let
C_{4},C_{5}>0
be some absolute constants and

C_{\zeta}=C_{4}(1+\vartheta_{2})\max_{m=1,\ldots,M}\rho_{x}^{1/2}\rho_{a}^{1/2}\{\rho_{b}^{1/2}\|\bm{\Sigma}^{m,\mathbf{u}}\|_{2}^{1/2}+(\rho_{b}\rho_{a})^{1/2}\|\bm{\Sigma}^{m,\mathbf{q}}\|_{2}^{1/2}+\|\bm{\Sigma}_{i}^{\mathbf{r}}\|_{2}^{1/2}+\|\bm{\Sigma}_{i}^{\mathbf{w}}\|_{2}^{1/2}\}.

Assume conditions of Lemma
D.2
hold and sample size
N=O(C_{\zeta}^{2}\{(\alpha^{\star}k)^{2}+\log p\})
. For any support set
\bar{{\mathcal{S}}}_{i}
defined in (
D.5
) satisfying
|\bar{{\mathcal{S}}}_{i}|\leq C_{5}k^{2}s^{\star}
, we have

\frac{1}{M}\|[\nabla_{\mathbf{B}_{i}}f(\mathbf{A}^{\star},\mathbf{B}^{\star})]_{\bar{\mathcal{S}}_{i}}\|_{r(k,\infty)}>C_{\zeta}\sqrt{\frac{(\alpha^{\star}k)^{2}+\log p}{N}},

with probability smaller than
p^{-1}\exp\{-(\alpha^{\star}k)^{2}\}
.

D.3
One-step Update

With results from Lemma
D.1
–
D.4
introduced in Section
D.2
as the building blocks, we are able to construct the analysis of the one-step update of Algorithm
1
. Noting that the sample complexity required in Lemma
D.1
–
D.4
depend on some condition numbers
\kappa_{m}
,
C_{\epsilon}
and
C_{\zeta}
. We assume that
\kappa_{m}^{2}
,
C_{\epsilon}^{2}
and
C_{\zeta}^{2}
are not large and see them as constants. Therefore, we could drop the terms and simplify the notations by focusing the rates of convergence with respect to
\alpha^{\star}
,
k
,
s^{\star}
,
p
,
M
and
k_{m}
for
m=1,\ldots,M
.
Lemma
D.5
states that with proper initial condition and step size
\eta_{A}
and
\eta_{B}
, the distance
\max_{i\in V}\|\mathbf{B}_{i}^{+}-\mathbf{B}_{i}^{\star}\|_{F}^{2}+\max_{m=1,\ldots,M}\|\mathbf{A}^{m+}-\mathbf{A}^{m\star}\|_{F}^{2}
is contracted at each iteration.

Lemma D.5
.

[One-Step Update] Suppose that the conditions of Lemma
D.2
hold.
Let

N=O(s^{\star}\max_{m=1,\ldots,M}k_{m}+(\alpha^{\star}k)^{2}+\log p+\log M)

. Define

\rho_{1}=4\left\{1-\frac{3p(s^{\star}+1)\Upsilon_{1}}{4\Upsilon_{2}}\eta_{A}\right\},\quad\rho_{2}=C_{\vartheta}\left(1-\frac{M\Upsilon_{6}}{4}\eta_{B}\right),

and
\delta_{0}:=\exp\{-(s^{\star}+1)\min_{m=1,\ldots,M}k_{m}\}\vee\exp\{-(\alpha^{\star}k)^{2}\}
.
Suppose that

\displaystyle\alpha^{\star}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\Upsilon_{1}\Upsilon_{6}}{32(1+\vartheta_{1})(1+\vartheta_{2})\tau_{2}\Upsilon_{2}\Upsilon_{3}^{2}},

\displaystyle\eta_{A}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{3p(s^{\star}+1)}\left(\frac{\Upsilon_{6}}{8\Upsilon_{5}}\wedge\frac{2}{\Upsilon_{2}}\right),\quad\eta_{B}\leq\frac{1}{12M}\left(\frac{\Upsilon_{1}}{\Upsilon_{5}\Upsilon_{2}}\wedge\frac{\Upsilon_{6}}{2\Upsilon_{7}}\right),

and
\eta_{A}
and
\eta_{B}
are selected such that
\rho_{1},\rho_{2}<1
and satisfy

4p\eta_{A}=C_{\vartheta}M\eta_{B}
.
Then,

\max_{i\in V}\|\mathbf{B}_{i}^{+}-\mathbf{B}_{i}^{\star}\|_{F}^{2}+\max_{m=1,\ldots,M}\|\mathbf{A}^{m+}-\mathbf{A}^{m\star}\|_{F}^{2}\\
\leq\rho_{1}\max_{m=1,\ldots,M}\|{\mathbf{A}^{m}}-\mathbf{A}^{m\star}\|_{F}^{2},+\rho_{2}\max_{i\in V}\|\mathbf{B}_{i}-\mathbf{B}_{i}^{\star}\|_{F}^{2}+\frac{\Upsilon_{8}}{\Upsilon_{1}}\max_{m=1,\ldots,M}\frac{k(k_{m}+k+\log M)}{N}\\
+\frac{\Upsilon_{9}}{\Upsilon_{7}}\frac{s^{\star}\{(\alpha^{\star}k)^{2}+\log p\}}{N},

with probability at least
1-10\delta_{0}
.

Proof of Lemma
D.5
.

First, we combine the results from Lemma
D.1
and Lemma
D.2
, we can obtain

\displaystyle\max_{i\in V}\|\mathbf{B}_{i}^{+}-\mathbf{B}_{i}^{\star}\|_{F}^{2}

\displaystyle+\max_{m=1,\ldots,M}\|\mathbf{A}^{m+}-\mathbf{A}^{m\star}\|_{F}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq T_{1}\max_{m=1,\ldots,M}\|{\mathbf{A}^{m}}-\mathbf{A}^{m\star}\|_{F}^{2}+T_{2}\max_{i\in V}\|\mathbf{B}_{i}-\mathbf{B}_{i}^{\star}\|_{F}^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+T_{3}\max_{m=1,\ldots,M}\|\nabla_{{\mathbf{A}^{m}}}f(\mathbf{A}^{\star},\mathbf{B}^{\star})\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+T_{4}\max_{i\in V}\|[\nabla_{\mathbf{B}_{i}}f(\mathbf{A}^{\star},\mathbf{B}^{\star})]_{\bar{\mathcal{S}}_{i}}\|^{2}_{r(k,\infty)}

(D.10)

where

\displaystyle T_{1}

\displaystyle=4\left\{1-\eta_{A}\frac{3p(s^{\star}+1)\Upsilon_{1}}{2\Upsilon_{2}}+\eta_{A}\frac{2ps^{\star}\Upsilon_{4}^{2}\Upsilon_{3}^{2}}{\Upsilon_{6}}\right\}+C_{\vartheta}Ms^{\star}\eta_{B}\left(\frac{2\Upsilon_{4}^{2}\Upsilon_{3}^{2}}{\Upsilon_{6}}+3\Upsilon_{5}M\eta_{B}\right);

\displaystyle T_{2}

\displaystyle=C_{\vartheta}{\left(1-\eta_{B}M\Upsilon_{6}+3\eta_{B}^{2}M^{2}\Upsilon_{7}\right)}+4p\eta_{A}\left(\frac{\Upsilon_{6}}{2}+3p(s^{\star}+1)\Upsilon_{5}\eta_{A}\right);

\displaystyle T_{3}

\displaystyle=4k\eta_{A}\left(\frac{4\Upsilon_{2}}{p(s^{\star}+1)\Upsilon_{1}}+3\eta_{A}\right);

\displaystyle T_{4}

\displaystyle=C_{\vartheta}(1+\vartheta_{1})s^{\star}\eta_{B}\left(\frac{2}{M\Upsilon_{6}}+3\eta_{B}\right).

First, we want to simplify the term of
T_{1}
. With the choices of
\alpha^{\star}
,
\eta_{B}
in the statement, we can obtain

\displaystyle\frac{2ps^{\star}\Upsilon_{4}^{2}\Upsilon_{3}^{2}}{\Upsilon_{6}}\leq\frac{p(s^{\star}+1)\Upsilon_{1}}{4\Upsilon_{2}},\quad\left(\frac{2\Upsilon_{4}^{2}\Upsilon_{3}^{2}}{\Upsilon_{6}}+3\Upsilon_{5}M\eta_{B}\right)\leq\frac{\Upsilon_{1}}{2\Upsilon_{2}}.

Since
4p\eta_{A}=C_{\vartheta}M\eta_{B}
, we can conclude

\displaystyle T_{1}\leq 4\left\{1-\frac{3p(s^{\star}+1)\Upsilon_{1}}{4\Upsilon_{2}}\eta_{A}\right\}.

Next, we want to simplify the term
T_{2}
. Similarly, with the choices of
\alpha^{\star}
,
\eta_{B}
, and
\eta_{A}
, we have

\displaystyle 3\eta_{B}M^{2}\Upsilon_{7}\leq\frac{M\Upsilon_{6}}{8},\quad 3p(s^{\star}+1)\Upsilon_{5}\eta_{A}\leq\frac{\Upsilon_{6}}{8};

Then, using the fact that
p\eta_{A}=C_{\vartheta}\eta_{B}
, we can conclude that

\displaystyle T_{2}\leq C_{\vartheta}\left\{1-\frac{M\Upsilon_{6}}{4}\eta_{B}\right\}.

With the choice of
\eta_{A}
, we have

\displaystyle T_{3}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{32}{3}\frac{k}{\Upsilon_{1}\{p(s^{\star}+1)\}^{2}}+\frac{16}{3}\frac{k}{\Upsilon_{2}^{2}\{p(s^{\star}+1)\}^{2}}.

Using the fact that
\Upsilon_{2}^{2}\geq 2\Upsilon_{1}
, we can further bound the above display as

\displaystyle T_{3}\leq\frac{14k}{\Upsilon_{1}\{p(s^{\star}+1)\}^{2}}\leq\frac{14k}{\Upsilon_{1}(ps^{\star})^{2}}.

Recall the definition of
\Upsilon_{8}
in Section
C
. In fact, by Lemma
D.3
and take the union bound over
m=1,\ldots,M
, we can verify that

\displaystyle T_{3}\max_{m=1,\ldots,M}\|\nabla_{{\mathbf{A}^{m}}}f(\mathbf{A}^{\star},\mathbf{B}^{\star})\|_{2}^{2}\leq\frac{\Upsilon_{8}}{\Upsilon_{1}}\max_{m=1,\ldots,M}k\frac{k+k_{m}+\log M}{N},

(D.11)

with probability at least
1-\max_{m=1,\ldots,M}\exp\{-(k_{m}+k)\}\geq 1-\delta_{0}
, where we define this event as
\mathcal{E}_{A}
.

We find the upper bound of
T_{4}
in a similar way as we do for
T_{3}
. Note that by Lemma
I.1
, We have
\Upsilon_{7}\geq\Upsilon_{6}^{2}
. Then, with the choice of
\eta_{B}
, we have

\displaystyle T_{4}\leq C_{\vartheta}(1+\vartheta_{1})s^{\star}\left(\frac{1}{12M^{2}\Upsilon_{7}}+\frac{3}{576}\frac{1}{M^{2}\Upsilon_{7}}\right)\leq\frac{C_{\vartheta}(1+\vartheta_{1})s^{\star}}{M^{2}\Upsilon_{7}}.

Recall the definition of
\Upsilon_{9}
in Section
C
.
Then, apply Lemma
D.4
and take the union bound over
i\in V
, we can obtain that

T_{4}\max_{i\in V}\|[\nabla_{\mathbf{B}_{i}}f(\mathbf{A}^{\star},\mathbf{B}^{\star})]_{\bar{\mathcal{S}}_{i}}\|_{r(k,\infty)}^{2}\leq\frac{\Upsilon_{9}}{\Upsilon_{7}}\frac{s^{\star}\{(\alpha^{\star}k)^{2}+\log p\}}{N},

(D.12)

with probability at least
1-\exp\{-(\alpha^{\star}k)^{2}\}\geq 1-\delta_{0}
and we define such event as
\mathcal{E}_{B}
.

Let
\mathcal{E}
be the event when Lemma
D.1
–
D.2
hold. Therefore, by De Morgan’s law,
\mathcal{E}\cap\mathcal{E}_{A}\cap\mathcal{E}_{B}
happens with probability at least

1-10\delta_{0}
.
∎

D.4
Proof of Theorem
5.3

First, we apply Lemma
D.5
for one iteration and obtain that with probability at least
1-10\delta_{0}
:

\max_{i\in V}\|\mathbf{B}_{i}^{(1)}-\mathbf{B}_{i}^{\star}\|_{F}^{2}+\max_{m=1,\ldots,M}\|\mathbf{A}^{m(1)}-\mathbf{A}^{m\star}\|_{F}^{2}\\
\leq\rho_{1}\max_{m=1,\ldots,M}\|\mathbf{A}^{m(0)}-\mathbf{A}^{m\star}\|_{F}^{2},+\rho_{2}\max_{i\in V}\|\mathbf{B}_{i}^{(0)}-\mathbf{B}_{i}^{\star}\|_{F}^{2}+\Xi^{2},

where the right hand side is bounded by
(C_{1}+1)(C_{A}^{2}\rho_{a}+C_{B}^{2}\rho_{b})
because (i)
\rho_{1},\rho_{2}<1
and (ii)
\Xi^{2}\leq C_{1}(C_{A}^{2}\rho_{a}+C_{B}^{2}\rho_{b})
under the condition of Theorem
5.3
. Therefore, we apply Lemma
D.5
again to obtain the upper bound for
\max_{i\in V}\|\mathbf{B}_{i}^{(2)}-\mathbf{B}_{i}^{\star}\|_{F}^{2}+\max_{m=1,\ldots,M}\|\mathbf{A}^{m(2)}-\mathbf{A}^{m\star}\|_{F}^{2}
:

\max_{i\in V}\|\mathbf{B}_{i}^{(2)}-\mathbf{B}_{i}^{\star}\|_{F}^{2}+\max_{m=1,\ldots,M}\|\mathbf{A}^{m(2)}-\mathbf{A}^{m\star}\|_{F}^{2}\\
\leq\rho_{1}\max_{m=1,\ldots,M}\|\mathbf{A}^{m(1)}-\mathbf{A}^{m\star}\|_{F}^{2},+\rho_{2}\max_{i\in V}\|\mathbf{B}_{i}^{(1)}-\mathbf{B}_{i}^{\star}\|_{F}^{2}+(1+\pi)\Xi^{2},

where
\pi=\rho_{1}\vee\rho_{2}
and with probability at least
1-10\delta_{0}
.

Then, iterate for
L
times and by the telescoping technique, we complete the proof:

\displaystyle\max_{m=1,\ldots,M}\|\mathbf{A}^{m(L)}-\mathbf{A}^{m\star}\|_{F}^{2}+\max_{i\in V}\|\mathbf{B}_{i}^{(L)}-\mathbf{B}_{i}^{\star}\|_{F}^{2}\leq\pi^{L}R_{0}^{2}+\frac{1}{1-\pi}\Xi^{2},

with probability at least
1-10\delta_{0}
.

D.5
Proof of Lemma
D.1
–
D.4

Proof of Lemma
D.1
.

We study one iteration for updating
{\mathbf{A}^{m}}
for
m=1,\ldots,M
via Algorithm
1
and we have

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{A}^{m+}-\mathbf{A}^{m\star}\|_{F}^{2}

\displaystyle={\|\mathcal{P}_{\tau}\left({\mathbf{A}^{m}}-\eta_{A}\nabla_{{\mathbf{A}^{m}}}f(\mathbf{A},\mathbf{B})\right)-\mathbf{A}^{m\star}\|_{F}^{2}}.

First, we claim that any row of
\mathbf{A}^{m\star}
is in
\mathcal{K}_{m}(\tau_{1},\tau_{2})
defined by (
3.8
) with probability at least
1-5\exp(-\sum_{m=1}^{2}k_{m})
. Then, apply Lemma
H.1
and the above display could be further bounded as

\displaystyle\leq 4{\|{\mathbf{A}^{m}}-\eta_{A}\nabla_{{\mathbf{A}^{m}}}f(\mathbf{A},\mathbf{B})-\mathbf{A}^{m\star}\|_{F}^{2}}

\displaystyle=4\{\|{\mathbf{A}^{m}}-\mathbf{A}^{m\star}\|_{F}^{2}-2\eta_{A}\mathcal{I}_{1,m}+\eta_{A}^{2}\mathcal{I}_{2,m}\},

where

\mathcal{I}_{1,m}=\langle{\nabla_{{\mathbf{A}^{m}}}f(\mathbf{A},\mathbf{B})},{{\mathbf{A}^{m}}-\mathbf{A}^{m\star}}\rangle,\quad\mathcal{I}_{2,m}=\|\nabla_{{\mathbf{A}^{m}}}f(\mathbf{A},\mathbf{B})\|_{F}^{2}.

Now we justify the claim that any row of
\mathbf{A}^{m\star}
is in
\mathcal{K}_{m}(\tau_{1},\tau_{2})
. For each row
j=1,\ldots,k
, we could apply triangle inequality and obtain that

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{A}_{j\cdot}^{m\star}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\|\mathbf{A}_{j\cdot}^{m\star}-\mathbf{A}_{j\cdot}^{m(0)}\|_{2}+\|\mathbf{A}_{j\cdot}^{m(0)}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\|\mathbf{A}_{j\cdot}^{m\star}-\mathbf{A}_{j\cdot}^{m(0)}\|_{2}+\|\mathbf{A}^{m(0)}\|_{2}.

Under Assumption
4
and
N=O(\max_{m=1,\ldots,M}\kappa_{m}^{2}k_{m})
, by Theorem
5.1
, we know that
\|\mathbf{A}_{j\cdot}^{m\star}-\mathbf{A}_{j\cdot}^{m(0)}\|_{2}
is bounded by some small constant with probability at least
1-5\exp(-\sum_{m=1}^{M}k_{m})
, and we define such event as
\mathcal{E}_{I}
. Therefore, there exists some finite
\tau_{2}>0
such that
\|\mathbf{A}_{j\cdot}^{m\star}\|_{2}\leq\tau_{2}k_{m}^{-1/2}\|\mathbf{A}^{m(0)}\|_{2}
for
j=1,\ldots,k
. The existence of a valid
\tau_{1}
can be justified in a similar way and hence we omit the
step.

In order to find the upper bound of
\|\mathbf{A}^{m+}-\mathbf{A}^{m\star}\|_{F}^{2}
, it suffices to find the lower bound of
\mathcal{I}_{1,m}
and the upper bound of
\mathcal{I}_{2,m}
, which are presented in the Lemma
D.6
and Lemma
D.7
, respectively.

On the event of
\mathcal{E}_{I}
, combining the results of Lemma
D.6
–
D.7
and taking the maximum over
m=1,\ldots,M
on both sides, we obtain that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4}\max_{m=1,\ldots,M}\|\mathbf{A}^{m+}-\mathbf{A}^{m\star}\|_{F}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\left(1-\eta_{A}\frac{3p(s^{\star}+1)\Upsilon_{1}}{2\Upsilon_{2}}+\eta_{A}\frac{2ps^{\star}\Upsilon_{4}^{2}\Upsilon_{3}^{2}}{\Upsilon_{6}}\right)\max_{m=1,\ldots,M}\|{\mathbf{A}^{m}}-\mathbf{A}^{m\star}\|_{F}^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+k\eta_{A}\left(\frac{4\Upsilon_{2}}{p(s^{\star}+1)\Upsilon_{1}}+3\eta_{A}\right)\max_{m=1,\ldots,M}\|\nabla_{{\mathbf{A}^{m}}}f(\mathbf{A}^{\star},\mathbf{B}^{\star})\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+p\eta_{A}\left(\frac{\Upsilon_{6}}{2}+3p(s^{\star}+1)\Upsilon_{5}\eta_{A}\right)\max_{i\in V}\|\mathbf{B}_{i}-\mathbf{B}_{i}^{\star}\|_{F}^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+\eta_{A}\left(-\frac{2}{p(s^{\star}+1)\Upsilon_{2}}+3\eta_{A}\right)

\displaystyle\quad\times\left\{\max_{m=1,\ldots,M}\|\nabla_{{\mathbf{A}^{m}}}f(\mathbf{A},\mathbf{B}^{\star})-\nabla_{{\mathbf{A}^{m}}}f(\mathbf{A}^{\star},\mathbf{B}^{\star})\|_{F}^{2}\right\}.

(D.13)

with probability at least
1-\max_{m=1,\ldots,M}\exp\{-k_{m}(s^{\star}+1)\}
.
By conditions of
\eta_{A}
,
-{2}/\{p(s^{\star}+1)\Upsilon_{2}\}+3\eta_{A}<0
and hence we can drop the last term of (
D.5
).

Then, marginalizing over the event
\mathcal{E}_{I}
, (
D.8
) holds with probability at least

\left\{1-5\exp\left(-\sum_{m=1}^{M}k_{m}\right)\right\}\left[1-\max_{m=1,\ldots,M}\exp\{-k_{m}(s^{\star}+1)\}\right]\\
\geq 1-\max_{m=1,\ldots,M}6\exp\{-k_{m}(s^{\star}+1)\}.

∎

Proof of Lemma
D.2
.

We study one iteration for updating
\mathbf{B}_{i}
for
i\in[p]
via Algorithm
1
.
Recall that
s=\vartheta_{1}s^{\star}/2
and
\alpha=\vartheta_{2}\alpha^{\star}/2
.
Then, apply Lemma
H.3
–
H.4
and (
D.7
), we have

\|\mathbf{B}_{i}^{+}-\mathbf{B}_{i}^{\star}\|_{F}^{2}=\|\mathcal{H}_{\alpha}\circ{\mathcal{T}}_{s}\left\{\mathbf{B}_{i}-\eta_{B}[\nabla_{\mathbf{B}_{i}}f(\mathbf{A},\mathbf{B})]_{\bar{\mathcal{S}}_{i}}\right\}-\mathbf{B}_{i}^{\star}\|_{F}^{2}\\
\leq C_{\vartheta}\big\{\|\mathbf{B}_{i}-\mathbf{B}_{i}^{\star}\|_{F}^{2}-2\eta_{B}\mathcal{I}_{3,i}+\eta_{B}^{2}\mathcal{I}_{4,i}\big\},

(D.14)

where

\mathcal{I}_{3,i}=\langle{[\nabla_{\mathbf{B}_{i}}f(\mathbf{A},\mathbf{B})]_{\bar{\mathcal{S}}_{i}}},{\mathbf{B}_{i}-\mathbf{B}_{i}^{\star}}\rangle,\quad\mathcal{I}_{4,i}=\|[\nabla_{\mathbf{B}_{i}}f(\mathbf{A},\mathbf{B})]_{\bar{\mathcal{S}}_{i}}\|_{F}^{2}.

Next, we will apply Lemma
D.8
for the lower bound of
\mathcal{I}_{3,i}
and apply Lemma
D.9
for the upper bound of
\mathcal{I}_{4,i}
for
i\in V
.

Combining Lemma
D.8
–
D.9
and taking the maximum over
i\in V
yields,

\displaystyle\max_{i\in V}\|\mathbf{B}_{i}^{+}-\mathbf{B}_{i}^{\star}\|_{F}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C_{\vartheta}\bigg\{\left(1-\eta_{B}M\Upsilon_{6}+3\eta_{B}^{2}M^{2}\Upsilon_{7}\right)\max_{i\in V}\|\mathbf{B}_{i}-\mathbf{B}_{i}^{\star}\|_{F}^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+Ms^{\star}\eta_{B}\left(\frac{2\Upsilon_{4}^{2}\Upsilon_{3}^{2}}{\Upsilon_{6}}+3\Upsilon_{5}M\eta_{B}\right)\max_{m=1,\ldots,M}\|{\mathbf{A}^{m}}-\mathbf{A}^{m\star}\|_{F}^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+(1+\vartheta_{1})s^{\star}\eta_{B}\left(\frac{2}{M\Upsilon_{6}}+3\eta_{B}\right)\max_{i\in V}\|[\nabla_{\mathbf{B}_{i}}f(\mathbf{A}^{\star},\mathbf{B}^{\star})]_{\bar{\mathcal{S}}_{i}}\|^{2}_{r(k,\infty)}\bigg\},

with probability at least
1-\max_{m=1,\ldots,M}\exp(-k_{m}s^{\star})
.

∎

Before showing the proofs of Lemma
D.3
–
D.4
, we introduce the notion of Orlicz norm, where we only consider the sub-exponential and sub-Gaussian cases here. Assume that
X
is a sub-exponential random variable, and sub-exponential norm is defined as

\displaystyle\|X\|_{\psi_{1}}=\inf\{t>0:\mathbb{E}\exp(|X|/t)\leq 2\}.

Assume that
X
is a sub-Gaussian random variable, and sub-Gaussian norm is defined as

\displaystyle\|X\|_{\psi_{2}}=\inf\{t>0:\mathbb{E}\exp(|X|^{2}/t^{2})\leq 2\}.

Proof of Lemma
D.3
.

Let
\mathcal{N}_{1}
be the
1/8
-net of
\mathbb{S}^{k-1}=\{\mathbf{v}\in\mathbb{R}^{k}:\|\mathbf{v}\|_{2}=1\}
and
\mathcal{N}_{2}
be the
1/8
-net of
\mathbb{S}^{k_{m}-1}=\{\mathbf{v}\in\mathbb{R}^{k_{m}}:\|\mathbf{v}\|_{2}=1\}
. Then, by Lemma
I.4
, we have

\displaystyle\|\nabla_{{\mathbf{A}^{m}}}f(\mathbf{A}^{\star},\mathbf{B}^{\star})\|_{2}

\displaystyle=\sup_{\mathbf{u}\in\mathbb{S}^{k-1}}\sup_{\mathbf{d}\in\mathbb{S}^{k_{m}-1}}\left|\mathbf{u}^{\top}\nabla_{{\mathbf{A}^{m}}}f(\mathbf{A}^{\star},\mathbf{B}^{\star})\mathbf{d}\right|

\displaystyle\leq 2\max_{\mathbf{u}\in\mathcal{N}_{1}}\max_{\mathbf{d}\in\mathcal{N}_{2}}\left|\mathbf{u}^{\top}\nabla_{{\mathbf{A}^{m}}}f(\mathbf{A}^{\star},\mathbf{B}^{\star})\mathbf{d}\right|

Define

\displaystyle\mathbf{v}_{i}^{m,(n)}

\displaystyle=\widetilde{\mathbf{B}}_{i}^{\star}(\mathbf{I}_{p}\otimes\mathbf{A}^{m\star})\mathbf{y}^{m,(n)}

\displaystyle=\mathbf{A}^{m\star}\mathbf{y}_{i}^{m,(n)}-\mathbf{B}_{i}^{\star}(\mathbf{I}_{p-1}\otimes\mathbf{A}^{m\star})\mathbf{y}_{\backslash\{i\}}^{m,(n)}

\displaystyle=\widetilde{\mathbf{B}}_{i}^{\star}\left\{{\mathbf{u}^{m}}^{(n)}+(\mathbf{I}_{p}\otimes{\mathbf{A}^{m\star}}){\mathbf{q}^{m}}^{(n)}\right\}+\mathbf{r}_{i}^{(n)}+\mathbf{w}_{i}^{(n)},

(D.15)

for
i\in V
,
m=1,\ldots,M
and
n=1,\ldots,N
.
Using (
D.3
), we can write

\displaystyle\nabla_{{\mathbf{A}^{m}}}f(\mathbf{A}^{\star},\mathbf{B}^{\star})

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=1}^{p}(\mathbf{e}_{j}^{\top}\otimes\mathbf{I}_{k})\left\{\sum_{i=1}^{p}\widetilde{\mathbf{B}}_{i}^{\star\top}\widetilde{\mathbf{B}}_{i}^{\star}(\mathbf{I}_{p}\otimes\mathbf{A}^{m\star})\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}^{m}\right\}(\mathbf{e}_{j}\otimes\mathbf{I}_{k_{m}})

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=1}^{p}(\mathbf{e}_{j}^{\top}\otimes\mathbf{I}_{k})\left[\sum_{i=1}^{p}\widetilde{\mathbf{B}}_{i}^{\star\top}\left\{\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=1}^{N}\mathbf{v}_{i}^{m,(n)}(\mathbf{y}^{m,(n)})^{\top}\right\}\right](\mathbf{e}_{j}\otimes\mathbf{I}_{k_{m}}).

Then, combining the above two results, we observe that for any
\mathbf{u}\in\mathcal{N}_{1}
and
\mathbf{d}\in\mathcal{N}_{2}

\mathbf{u}^{\top}\left[\sum_{j=1}^{p}(\mathbf{e}_{j}^{\top}\otimes\mathbf{I}_{k})\left\{\sum_{i=1}^{p}\widetilde{\mathbf{B}}_{i}^{\star\top}\mathbf{v}_{i}^{m}(\mathbf{y}_{i}^{m})^{\top}\right\}(\mathbf{e}_{j}\otimes\mathbf{I}_{k_{m}})\right]\mathbf{d},

is a sub-exponential random variable. Therefore, we can obtain the upper bound of Orlicz norm as

\displaystyle\bigg\|\sum_{j=1}^{p}(\mathbf{e}_{j}^{\top}\otimes\mathbf{I}_{k})

\displaystyle\left\{\sum_{i=1}^{p}\widetilde{\mathbf{B}}_{i}^{\star\top}\mathbf{v}_{i}^{m}(\mathbf{y}_{i}^{m})^{\top}\right\}(\mathbf{e}_{j}\otimes\mathbf{I}_{k_{m}})\bigg\|_{\psi_{1}}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i=1}^{p}\bigg\|\sum_{j=1}^{p}(\mathbf{e}_{j}^{\top}\otimes\mathbf{I}_{k})\left\{\widetilde{\mathbf{B}}_{i}^{\star\top}\mathbf{v}_{i}^{m}(\mathbf{y}_{i}^{m})^{\top}\right\}(\mathbf{e}_{j}\otimes\mathbf{I}_{k_{m}})\bigg\|_{\psi_{1}}.

Note that if
j\not\in\mathscr{N}_{i}^{\star}\cup\{i\}
, then
(\mathbf{e}_{j}^{\top}\otimes\mathbf{I}_{k})\widetilde{\mathbf{B}}_{i}^{\star\top}={\bf 0}
. Therefore, the above display is equivalent as

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{p}\bigg\|\sum_{j\in\mathscr{N}_{i}^{\star}\cup\{i\}}(\mathbf{e}_{j}^{\top}\otimes\mathbf{I}_{k})\left\{\widetilde{\mathbf{B}}_{i}^{\star\top}\mathbf{v}_{i}^{m}(\mathbf{y}_{i}^{m})^{\top}\right\}(\mathbf{e}_{j}\otimes\mathbf{I}_{k_{m}})\bigg\|_{\psi_{1}}.

By Lemma 2.7.7 in
(
Vershynin 2018
)
, we can further bound the above display as

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i=1}^{p}\sum_{j\in\mathscr{N}_{i}^{\star}\cup\{i\}}\bigg\|(\mathbf{e}_{j}^{\top}\otimes\mathbf{I}_{k})\widetilde{\mathbf{B}}_{i}^{\star\top}\mathbf{v}_{i}^{m}\bigg\|_{\psi_{2}}\bigg\|(\mathbf{y}_{i}^{m})^{\top}(\mathbf{e}_{j}\otimes\mathbf{I}_{k_{m}})\bigg\|_{\psi_{2}}.

(D.16)

Using the fact that
|\mathscr{N}_{i}^{\star}\cup\{i\}|\leq s^{\star}+1
and the fact that

\displaystyle\|\widetilde{\mathbf{B}}_{i}^{\star\top}\mathbf{v}_{i}^{m}\|_{\psi_{2}}

\displaystyle=\bigg\|\widetilde{\mathbf{B}}_{i}^{\star\top}\left\{\widetilde{\mathbf{B}}_{i}^{\star}\left({\mathbf{u}^{m}}+(\mathbf{I}_{p}\otimes{\mathbf{A}^{m\star}}){\mathbf{q}^{m}}\right)+\mathbf{r}_{i}+\mathbf{w}_{i}\right\}\bigg\|_{\psi_{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\|\widetilde{\mathbf{B}}_{i}^{\star\top}\widetilde{\mathbf{B}}_{i}^{\star}{\mathbf{u}^{m}}\|_{\psi_{2}}+\|\widetilde{\mathbf{B}}_{i}^{\star\top}(\mathbf{I}_{p}\otimes{\mathbf{A}^{m\star}}){\mathbf{q}^{m}}\|_{\psi_{2}}+\|\widetilde{\mathbf{B}}_{i}^{\star\top}\mathbf{r}_{i}\|_{\psi_{2}}+\|\widetilde{\mathbf{B}}_{i}^{\star\top}\mathbf{w}_{i}\|_{\psi_{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\|\widetilde{\mathbf{B}}_{i}^{\star}\|_{2}(\|\widetilde{\mathbf{B}}_{i}^{\star}\|_{2}\|\bm{\Sigma}^{m,\mathbf{u}}\|_{2}^{1/2}+\|\widetilde{\mathbf{B}}_{i}^{\star}\|_{2}\|\mathbf{A}^{m\star}\|_{2}\|\bm{\Sigma}^{m,\mathbf{q}}\|_{2}^{1/2}+\|\bm{\Sigma}_{i}^{\mathbf{r}}\|_{2}^{1/2}+\|\bm{\Sigma}_{i}^{\mathbf{w}}\|_{2}^{1/2}),

for
i\in V
, we can further bound the right hand side of (
D.16
) as

\displaystyle\bigg\|\sum_{j=1}^{p}(\mathbf{e}_{j}^{\top}\otimes\mathbf{I}_{k})

\displaystyle\left\{\sum_{i=1}^{p}\widetilde{\mathbf{B}}_{i}^{\star\top}\mathbf{v}_{i}^{m}(\mathbf{y}_{i}^{m})^{\top}\right\}(\mathbf{e}_{j}\otimes\mathbf{I}_{k_{m}})\bigg\|_{\psi_{1}}

\displaystyle\leq p(s^{\star}+1)\|\bm{\Sigma}^{m}\|_{2}^{1/2}\max_{i\in V}\|\widetilde{\mathbf{B}}_{i}^{\star}\|_{2}(\|\widetilde{\mathbf{B}}_{i}^{\star}\|_{2}\|\bm{\Sigma}^{m,\mathbf{u}}\|_{2}^{1/2}+\|\widetilde{\mathbf{B}}_{i}^{\star}\|_{2}\|\mathbf{A}^{m\star}\|\|\bm{\Sigma}^{m,\mathbf{q}}\|_{2}^{1/2}

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad+\|\bm{\Sigma}_{i}^{\mathbf{r}}\|_{2}^{1/2}+\|\bm{\Sigma}_{i}^{\mathbf{w}}\|_{2}^{1/2})

\displaystyle\leq p(s^{\star}+1)\rho_{x}^{1/2}\rho_{b}^{1/2}\big\{\rho_{b}^{1/2}\|\bm{\Sigma}^{m,\mathbf{u}}\|_{2}^{1/2}+(\rho_{b}\rho_{a})^{1/2}\|\bm{\Sigma}^{m,\mathbf{q}}\|_{2}^{1/2}

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad+\max_{i\in V}(\|\bm{\Sigma}_{i}^{\mathbf{r}}\|_{2}^{1/2}+\|\bm{\Sigma}_{i}^{\mathbf{w}}\|_{2}^{1/2})\big\},

where the last line follows by Assumption
6
–
7
.

Next, let
C_{\epsilon}=C_{3}\max_{i\in V}\rho_{x}^{1/2}\rho_{b}^{1/2}\big\{\rho_{b}^{1/2}\|\bm{\Sigma}^{m,\mathbf{u}}\|_{2}^{1/2}+(\rho_{b}\rho_{a})^{1/2}\|\bm{\Sigma}^{m,\mathbf{q}}\|_{2}^{1/2}+\|\bm{\Sigma}_{i}^{\mathbf{r}}\|_{2}^{1/2}+\|\bm{\Sigma}_{i}^{\mathbf{w}}\|_{2}^{1/2}\big\}

for some constant
C_{3}>0
.
Under the condition of the sample size
N=O(C_{\epsilon}^{2}(k+k_{m}+\log M))
,
apply Bernstein’s inequality (see Theorem 2.8.1 in
Vershynin 2018
) and take the union bound over
\mathcal{N}_{1}
and
\mathcal{N}_{2}
, where
\left|\mathcal{N}_{1}\right|\leq 17^{k}
and
\left|\mathcal{N}_{2}\right|\leq 17^{k_{m}}
, we can obtain

\frac{1}{p(s^{\star}+1)}\|\nabla_{{\mathbf{A}^{m}}}f(\mathbf{A}^{\star},\mathbf{B}^{\star})\|_{2}\geq C_{\epsilon}\sqrt{\frac{k_{m}+k+\log M}{N}},

with probability smaller than
M^{-1}\exp\{-(k_{m}+k)\}
.
∎

Proof of Lemma
D.4
.

We first expand
\nabla_{\mathbf{B}_{i}}f(\mathbf{A}^{\star},\mathbf{B}^{\star})
and obtains

\nabla_{\mathbf{B}_{i}}f(\mathbf{A}^{\star},\mathbf{B}^{\star})=-\frac{1}{N}\sum_{m=1}^{M}\sum_{n=1}^{N}\{\mathbf{A}^{m\star}\mathbf{y}_{i}^{m,(n)}-\mathbf{B}_{i}^{\star}(\mathbf{I}_{p-1}\otimes{\mathbf{A}^{m\star}})\mathbf{y}_{\backslash\{i\}}^{m,(n)}\}(\mathbf{y}_{\backslash\{i\}}^{m,(n)})^{\top}(\mathbf{I}_{p-1}\otimes{\mathbf{A}^{m\star\top}}).

Replacing

\mathbf{A}^{m\star}\mathbf{y}_{i}^{m,(n)}-\mathbf{B}_{i}^{\star}(\mathbf{I}_{p-1}\otimes{\mathbf{A}^{m\star}})\mathbf{y}_{\backslash\{i\}}^{m,(n)}=\mathbf{v}_{i}^{m,(n)},

for
n=1,\ldots,N
and
m=1,\ldots,M
yields

\nabla_{\mathbf{B}_{i}}f(\mathbf{A}^{\star},\mathbf{B}^{\star})=-\frac{1}{N}\sum_{m=1}^{M}\sum_{n=1}^{N}\mathbf{v}_{i}^{m,(n)}(\mathbf{y}_{\backslash\{i\}}^{m,(n)})^{\top}(\mathbf{I}_{p-1}\otimes{\mathbf{A}^{m\star\top}}).

Define
\mathbf{t}^{m,(n)}_{i}\in\mathbb{R}^{k\times(p-1)}
which transforms the original matrix

(\mathbf{y}_{\backslash\{i\}}^{m,(n)})^{\top}(\mathbf{I}_{p-1}\otimes{\mathbf{A}^{m\star\top}})\in\mathbb{R}^{1\times k(p-1)}
to

\mathbf{t}^{m,(n)}_{i}=(\mathbf{A}^{m\star}\mathbf{y}_{1}^{m,(n)},\ldots,\mathbf{A}^{m\star}\mathbf{y}_{p}^{m,(n)})\in\mathbb{R}^{k\times(p-1)},

(D.17)

for
m=1,\ldots,M
and
n=1,\ldots,N
. Then, we can write

\bigg\|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{m=1}^{M}\sum_{n=1}^{N}[\mathbf{v}_{i}^{m,(n)}(\mathbf{y}_{\backslash\{i\}}^{m,(n)})^{\top}(\mathbf{I}_{p-1}\otimes{\mathbf{A}^{m\star\top}})]_{\bar{\mathcal{S}}_{i}}\bigg\|_{r(k,\infty)}=\bigg\|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{m=1}^{M}\sum_{n=1}^{N}[\mathbf{v}_{i}^{m,(n)}\otimes\mathbf{t}^{m,(n)}_{i}]_{\bar{\mathcal{S}}_{i}^{\prime}}\bigg\|_{2,\infty},

where we denote
\bar{\mathcal{S}}_{i}^{\prime}
as the support set whose elements are translated from
\bar{\mathcal{S}}_{i}
due to the transformation from a matrix of dimension
k\times k(p-1)
to
{k^{2}\times(p-1)}
.

Step 1
.
Consider a fixed
\bar{{\mathcal{S}}}_{i}
and
recall the definition of the neighborhood set
\bar{\mathscr{N}}_{i}
and the relation of
\bar{\mathcal{S}}_{i}
and
\bar{\mathscr{N}}_{i}
defined in (
D.2
).
Since
|\bar{\mathscr{N}}_{i}|\leq(1+\vartheta_{1})s^{\star}
, we know that there are at most
(1+\vartheta_{1})s^{\star}
nonzero columns in

\sum_{m=1}^{M}\sum_{n=1}^{N}[\mathbf{v}_{i}^{m,(n)}\otimes\mathbf{t}^{m,(n)}_{i}]_{\bar{\mathcal{S}}_{i}^{\prime}}
.
Similarly, for each column of
\sum_{m=1}^{M}\sum_{n=1}^{N}[\mathbf{v}_{i}^{m,(n)}\otimes\mathbf{t}^{m,(n)}_{i}]_{\bar{\mathcal{S}}_{i}^{\prime}}
, there are at most
\{(1+\vartheta_{2})\alpha^{\star}k\}^{2}
nonzero entries.
Define the subset of the support set

\displaystyle{\mathcal{S}}_{\cdot,i}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\{u:(u,i)\in{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}\};

\displaystyle{\mathcal{S}}_{\cdot,(i-1)k+1:ik}

\displaystyle=\{(u,v):(u,v)\in{\mathcal{S}},v=(i-1)k+1,\ldots,ik\}.

Let

\displaystyle\mathcal{U}({\mathcal{S}}^{\prime})

\displaystyle=\{\mathbf{u}\in R^{k^{2}}:\text{supp}{(\mathbf{u})}\subset\{{\mathcal{S}}_{\cdot,i}^{\prime},i=1,\ldots,(p-1)\},\|\mathbf{u}\|_{2}\leq 1\};

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}({\mathcal{S}})

\displaystyle=\{\mathbf{D}\in\mathbb{R}^{k\times k}:\text{supp}(\mathbf{D})\subset\{{\mathcal{S}}_{\cdot,(i-1)k+1:ik},i=1,\ldots,(p-1)\},\|\mathbf{D}\|_{F}\leq 1\}.

Therefore, we can express the above equation as

\displaystyle\bigg\|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{m=1}^{M}\sum_{n=1}^{N}[\mathbf{v}_{i}^{m,(n)}\otimes\mathbf{t}^{m,(n)}_{i}]_{\bar{\mathcal{S}}_{i}^{\prime}}\bigg\|_{2,\infty}

\displaystyle=\max_{j\in\bar{\mathscr{N}}_{i}}\sup_{\mathbf{u}\in\mathcal{U}(\bar{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}_{i})}\left|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{m=1}^{M}\sum_{n=1}^{N}\mathbf{u}^{\top}[\mathbf{v}_{i}^{m,(n)}\otimes\mathbf{t}^{m,(n)}_{i}]_{\bar{\mathcal{S}}_{i}^{\prime}}\mathbf{e}_{j}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\max_{j\in\bar{\mathscr{N}}_{i}}\sup_{\mathbf{u}\in\mathcal{U}(\bar{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}_{i})}\left|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{m=1}^{M}\sum_{n=1}^{N}\mathbf{u}^{\top}\mathbf{v}_{i}^{m,(n)}\otimes\mathbf{t}^{m,(n)}_{i}\mathbf{e}_{j}\right|

(D.18)

where
\mathbf{e}_{j}\in\mathbb{R}^{p-1}
denotes the
j
-th standard canonical basis.

For matrices
\mathbf{E}
,
\mathbf{F}
,
\mathbf{G}
,
\mathbf{H}
, applying the fact

\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}(\mathbf{E}^{\top}\mathbf{F}\mathbf{G}\mathbf{H}^{\top})=\VEC\left(\mathbf{E}\right)^{\top}(\mathbf{H}\otimes\mathbf{F})\VEC\left(\mathbf{G}\right)
to the right hand side of (
D.5
) we can obtain

\displaystyle\max_{j\in\bar{\mathscr{N}}_{i}}\sup_{\mathbf{u}\in\mathcal{U}(\bar{\mathcal{S}}_{i}^{\prime})}\frac{1}{N}

\displaystyle\sum_{m=1}^{M}\sum_{n=1}^{N}\mathbf{u}^{\top}\mathbf{v}_{i}^{m,(n)}\otimes\mathbf{t}^{m,(n)}_{i}\mathbf{e}_{j}

\displaystyle=\max_{j\in\bar{\mathscr{N}}_{i}}\sup_{\mathbf{D}\in\mathcal{D}(\bar{\mathcal{S}}_{i})}\frac{1}{N}\sum_{m=1}^{M}\sum_{n=1}^{N}\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}\left\{\mathbf{D}^{\top}\mathbf{t}^{m,(n)}_{i}\mathbf{e}_{j}\mathbf{v}_{i}^{m,(n)\top}\right\}

\displaystyle=\max_{j\in\bar{\mathscr{N}}_{i}}\sup_{\mathbf{D}\in\mathcal{D}(\bar{\mathcal{S}}_{i})}\frac{1}{N}\sum_{m=1}^{M}\sum_{n=1}^{N}(\mathbf{D}\mathbf{v}_{i}^{m,(n)})^{\top}\mathbf{t}^{m,(n)}_{i}\mathbf{e}_{j}.

Define
\mathcal{N}
to be the
\frac{1}{2}
-net of the set
\mathcal{D}(\bar{\mathcal{S}}_{i})
, we have

\max_{j\in\bar{\mathscr{N}}_{i}}\sup_{\mathbf{D}\in\mathcal{D}}\frac{1}{N}\sum_{m=1}^{M}\sum_{n=1}^{N}(\mathbf{D}\mathbf{v}_{i}^{m,(n)})^{\top}\mathbf{t}^{m,(n)}_{i}\mathbf{e}_{j}\leq 2\max_{j\in\bar{\mathscr{N}}_{i}}\max_{\mathbf{D}\in\mathcal{N}}\frac{1}{N}\sum_{m=1}^{M}\sum_{n=1}^{N}(\mathbf{D}\mathbf{v}_{i}^{m,(n)})^{\top}\mathbf{t}^{m,(n)}_{i}\mathbf{e}_{j}.

We consider the case that
elements of
\{\{\mathbf{y}^{m,(n)}\}_{m=1}^{M}\}_{n=1}^{N}
are pairwise independent. This implies that
(\mathbf{D}\mathbf{v}_{i}^{m,(n)})^{\top}\mathbf{t}^{m,(n)}_{i}\mathbf{e}_{j}
and
(\mathbf{D}\mathbf{v}_{i}^{m^{\prime},(n^{\prime})})^{\top}\mathbf{y}^{m^{\prime},(n^{\prime})}_{i}\mathbf{e}_{j}
are pairwise independent for every
n\neq n^{\prime}
. Given
n
being fixed,
(\mathbf{D}\mathbf{v}_{i}^{m,(n)})^{\top}\mathbf{t}^{m,(n)}_{i}\mathbf{e}_{j}
and
(\mathbf{D}\mathbf{v}_{i}^{m^{\prime},(n)})^{\top}\mathbf{y}^{m^{\prime},(n)}_{i}\mathbf{e}_{j}
might not be independent for
m\neq m^{\prime}
. Observe that

(\mathbf{D}\mathbf{v}_{i}^{m,(n)})^{\top}\mathbf{t}^{m,(n)}_{i}\mathbf{e}_{j}
are sub-exponentially distributed. Under this setting, we can bound the Orlicz norm
\|\cdot\|_{\psi_{1}}
of the random variable
M^{-1}\sum_{m=1}^{M}(\mathbf{D}\mathbf{v}_{i}^{m})^{\top}\mathbf{t}_{i}^{m}\mathbf{e}_{j}
as the following. By writing
\mathbf{v}_{i}^{m}
in terms of (
D.15
) and
\mathbf{t}_{i}
in terms of (
D.17
), we have

\displaystyle\bigg\|\frac{1}{M}\sum_{m=1}^{M}

\displaystyle(\mathbf{D}\mathbf{v}_{i}^{m})^{\top}\mathbf{t}_{i}^{m}\mathbf{e}_{j}\bigg\|_{\psi_{1}}

\displaystyle\leq\max_{m=1,\ldots,M}\|\mathbf{A}^{m\star}\|_{2}\|\bm{\Sigma}^{m}\|_{2}^{1/2}\big(\|\widetilde{\mathbf{B}}_{i}^{\star}\|_{2}\|\bm{\Sigma}^{m,\mathbf{u}}\|_{2}^{1/2}+\|\widetilde{\mathbf{B}}_{i}^{\star}\|_{2}\|\mathbf{A}^{m\star}\|\|\bm{\Sigma}^{m,\mathbf{q}}\|_{2}^{1/2}

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad+\|\bm{\Sigma}_{i}^{\mathbf{r}}\|_{2}^{1/2}+\|\bm{\Sigma}_{i}^{\mathbf{w}}\|_{2}^{1/2}\big)

\displaystyle\leq\max_{m=1,\ldots,M}\rho_{x}^{1/2}\rho_{a}^{1/2}\{\rho_{b}^{1/2}\|\bm{\Sigma}^{m,\mathbf{u}}\|_{2}^{1/2}+(\rho_{b}\rho_{a})^{1/2}\|\bm{\Sigma}^{m,\mathbf{q}}\|_{2}^{1/2}+\|\bm{\Sigma}_{i}^{\mathbf{r}}\|_{2}^{1/2}+\|\bm{\Sigma}_{i}^{\mathbf{w}}\|_{2}^{1/2}\},

where we recall that
\|\mathbf{B}_{i}^{\star}\|\leq\rho_{b}^{1/2}
for
i\in V
,
\|\mathbf{A}^{m\star}\|\leq\rho_{a}^{1/2}
and
\|\bm{\Sigma}^{m}\|\leq\rho_{x}
for
m=1,\ldots,M
.

Step 2
. We define
C_
ζ
= (1+
ϑ
_2)C_4max_m=1,…,M
ρ
_x^1/2
ρ
_a^1/2
{

ρ
_b^1/2∥
Σ
^m,
u
∥_2^1/2
+
(
ρ
_b
ρ
_a)^1/2∥
Σ
^m,
q
∥_2^1/2
+
∥
Σ
_i^
r
∥_2^1/2
+
∥
Σ
_i^
w
∥_2^1/2
}, for some small constant
C_{4}>0
.
Then, under the condition of the sample size
N=O(C_{\zeta}^{2}\{(\alpha^{\star}k)^{2}+\log p\})
, apply the Bernstein’s inequality with

t=C_{\zeta}\sqrt{\frac{(\alpha^{\star}k)^{2}+\log p}{N}},

and take the union bound over
j\in\bar{\mathscr{N}}_{i}
with
|\bar{\mathscr{N}}_{i}|\leq(1+\vartheta_{1})s^{\star}\leq p
and
\mathbf{D}\in\mathcal{N}
with
|\mathcal{N}|\leq 5^{(1+\vartheta_{2})^{2}(\alpha^{\star})^{2}k^{2}}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\bigg\{\frac{1}{M}\bigg\|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{m=1}^{M}\sum_{n=1}^{N}

\displaystyle[\mathbf{v}_{i}^{m,(n)}\otimes\mathbf{t}^{m,(n)}_{i}]_{\bar{\mathcal{S}}_{i}^{\prime}}\bigg\|_{2,\infty}>t\bigg\}

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\left\{2\max_{j\in\bar{\mathscr{N}}_{i}}\max_{\mathbf{D}\in\mathcal{N}}\frac{1}{MN}\sum_{m=1}^{M}\sum_{n=1}^{N}(\mathbf{D}\mathbf{v}_{i}^{m,(n)})^{\top}\mathbf{t}^{m,(n)}_{i}\mathbf{e}_{j}>t\right\}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j\in\bar{\mathscr{N}}_{i}}\sum_{\mathbf{D}\in\mathcal{N}}\mathbb{P}\left\{2\frac{1}{MN}\sum_{m=1}^{M}\sum_{n=1}^{N}(\mathbf{D}\mathbf{v}_{i}^{m,(n)})^{\top}\mathbf{t}^{m,(n)}_{i}\mathbf{e}_{j}>t\right\}

\displaystyle\leq p^{-1}\exp\{-(\alpha^{\star}k)^{2}\}.

(D.19)

∎

D.6
Lemma
D.6
–
D.9
and Their Proofs

In this section we introduce Lemma
D.6
–
D.9
in order. The proof is stated following to the introduction of each lemma. We start with stating Lemma
D.6
.

Lemma D.6
.

Let
\Upsilon_{1},\Upsilon_{2},\Upsilon_{3},\Upsilon_{4},\Upsilon_{6}
be constants defined in Section
C
.
Under the conditions of Lemma
D.1
, we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{1,m}

\displaystyle\geq\left(\frac{3p(s^{\star}+1)\Upsilon_{1}}{4\Upsilon_{2}}-\frac{ps^{\star}\Upsilon_{4}^{2}\Upsilon_{3}^{2}}{\Upsilon_{6}}\right)\|{\mathbf{A}^{m}}-\mathbf{A}^{m\star}\|_{F}^{2}-\frac{\Upsilon_{6}}{4}\sum_{i=1}^{p}\|\mathbf{B}_{i}-\mathbf{B}_{i}^{\star}\|_{F}^{2}

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{2k\Upsilon_{2}}{p(s^{\star}+1)\Upsilon_{1}}\|\nabla_{{\mathbf{A}^{m}}}f(\mathbf{A}^{\star},\mathbf{B}^{\star})\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{p(s^{\star}+1)\Upsilon_{2}}\|\nabla_{{\mathbf{A}^{m}}}f(\mathbf{A},\mathbf{B}^{\star})-\nabla_{{\mathbf{A}^{m}}}f(\mathbf{A}^{\star},\mathbf{B}^{\star})\|_{2}^{2},

with probability at least
1-M^{-1}\exp\{-k_{m}(s^{\star}+1)\}
.

Proof of Lemma
D.6
.

Let
\mathscr{M}_{i}^{\star}=\mathscr{N}_{i}^{\star}\cup\{i\}
, where
\mathscr{N}_{i}^{\star}
is defined in (
2.7
), with
\left|\mathscr{M}_{i}\right|\leq s^{\star}+1
and let

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{i}^{m}(\mathscr{M}_{i}^{\star})=\left\{\frac{\nu_{x}}{2}\leq\sigma_{\min}(\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}^{m}_{\mathscr{M}_{i}^{\star}\mathscr{M}_{i}^{\star}})\leq\sigma_{\max}(\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}^{m}_{\mathscr{M}_{i}^{\star}\mathscr{M}_{i}^{\star}})\leq\frac{3\rho_{x}}{2}\right\}.

(D.20)

Under the conditions of the lemma, we have

\mathbb{P}\left(\bigcap_{i=1}^{p}\mathcal{E}_{i}^{m}\right)\geq 1-M^{-1}\exp\{-k_{m}(s^{\star}+1)\},

using Lemma
I.2
.
We will work on the event

\mathcal{E}^{m}=\bigcap_{i=1}^{p}\mathcal{E}_{i}^{m}.

(D.21)

By definition of
\mathcal{I}_{1,m}
we can write

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{1,m}

\displaystyle=\langle{\nabla_{{\mathbf{A}^{m}}}f(\mathbf{A},\mathbf{B})},{{\mathbf{A}^{m}}-\mathbf{A}^{m\star}}\rangle

\displaystyle=\langle{\nabla_{{\mathbf{A}^{m}}}f(\mathbf{A},\mathbf{B})-\nabla_{{\mathbf{A}^{m}}}f(\mathbf{A},\mathbf{B}^{\star})},{{\mathbf{A}^{m}}-\mathbf{A}^{m\star}}\rangle

\displaystyle\quad+\langle{\nabla_{{\mathbf{A}^{m}}}f(\mathbf{A},\mathbf{B}^{\star})-\nabla_{{\mathbf{A}^{m}}}f(\mathbf{A}^{\star},\mathbf{B}^{\star})},{{\mathbf{A}^{m}}-\mathbf{A}^{m\star}}\rangle

\displaystyle\quad+\langle{\nabla_{{\mathbf{A}^{m}}}f(\mathbf{A}^{\star},\mathbf{B}^{\star})},{{\mathbf{A}^{m}}-\mathbf{A}^{m\star}}\rangle

\displaystyle=\mathcal{I}_{11,m}+\mathcal{I}_{12,m}+\mathcal{I}_{13,m}.

We proceed to lower-bound the terms separately.

Using (
D.2
), for
\mathcal{I}_{11,m}
we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{11,m}

\displaystyle=\langle{\nabla_{{\mathbf{A}^{m}}}f(\mathbf{A},\mathbf{B})-\nabla_{{\mathbf{A}^{m}}}f(\mathbf{A},\mathbf{B}^{\star})},{{\mathbf{A}^{m}}-\mathbf{A}^{m\star}}\rangle

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{p}\langle{(\widetilde{\mathbf{B}}_{i}^{\top}\widetilde{\mathbf{B}}_{i}-\widetilde{\mathbf{B}}_{i}^{\star\top}\widetilde{\mathbf{B}}_{i}^{\star})(\mathbf{I}_{p}\otimes{\mathbf{A}^{m}})\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}^{m}},{\mathbf{I}_{p}\otimes({\mathbf{A}^{m}}-\mathbf{A}^{m\star})}\rangle

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{p}\langle{\widetilde{\mathbf{B}}_{i}-\widetilde{\mathbf{B}}_{i}^{\star}},{\widetilde{\mathbf{B}}_{i}^{\star}\{\mathbf{I}_{p}\otimes{({\mathbf{A}^{m}}-\mathbf{A}^{m\star})}\}\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}^{m}(\mathbf{I}_{p}\otimes{\mathbf{A}^{m\top}})}\rangle

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{i=1}^{p}\langle{\widetilde{\mathbf{B}}_{i}-\widetilde{\mathbf{B}}_{i}^{\star}},{\widetilde{\mathbf{B}}_{i}(\mathbf{I}_{p}\otimes{{\mathbf{A}^{m}}})\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}^{m}\{\mathbf{I}_{p}\otimes{(\mathbf{A}^{m\top}-\mathbf{A}^{m\star\top})}\}}\rangle.

(D.22)

Let

\bar{{\mathcal{S}}}_{i,u\cdot}=\{v:(u,v)\in\bar{{\mathcal{S}}}_{i}\}
, where
\bar{{\mathcal{S}}}_{i}
is defined in (
D.7
). By definition, for each
u=1,\ldots,k
,
\left|\bar{{\mathcal{S}}}_{i,u\cdot}\right|\leq(1+\vartheta_{1})s^{\star}(1+\vartheta_{2})\alpha^{\star}k
. For the first term in (
D.22
), we apply the fact that the
i
-th block matrix
\mathbf{B}_{ii}=\mathbf{I}_{k}\in\mathbb{R}^{k\times k}
in
\widetilde{\mathbf{B}}_{i}
and
\mathbf{B}_{ii}^{\star}=\mathbf{I}_{k}\in\mathbb{R}^{k\times k}
in
\widetilde{\mathbf{B}}_{i}^{\star}
are equivalent. Hence the
i
-th block matrix
\widetilde{\mathbf{B}}_{i}-\widetilde{\mathbf{B}}_{i}^{\star}
is a zero matrix. Therefore, we can write

\displaystyle\langle

\displaystyle{\widetilde{\mathbf{B}}_{i}-\widetilde{\mathbf{B}}_{i}^{\star}},{\widetilde{\mathbf{B}}_{i}^{\star}\{\mathbf{I}_{p}\otimes{({\mathbf{A}^{m}}-\mathbf{A}^{m\star})}\}\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}^{m}(\mathbf{I}_{p}\otimes{\mathbf{A}^{m\top}})}\rangle
